]
.

1.6.
The Nairian cardinals

Let
{\mathcal{H}}
represent the class of pairs of ordinals
(\alpha,\beta)
such that the
\alpha
th element of the standard definability order of
{\rm{HOD}}
is an element of the
\beta
th.
(This is merely a technical convenience that allows us to give a concise statement of results from the second part of this paper; the only property of
{\mathcal{H}}
we use in the first part of the paper is that it is a definable class of pairs of ordinals.)

Given an ordinal
\gamma
, we write
{\mathcal{H}}\restriction\gamma
for
{\mathcal{H}}\cap(\gamma\times\gamma)

and
{\mathrm{N}}_{\gamma}^{-}
for the structure
L_{\gamma}({\mathcal{H}},\cup_{\beta<\gamma}\beta^{\omega})
, which is constructed relative to the predicate
{\mathcal{H}}
, adding (for each ordinal
\alpha<\gamma
) all
\omega
-sequences from
\alpha
at stage
\alpha+1
.
Note that
\gamma
is the ordinal height of this structure.

Let
{\mathrm{N}}_{\gamma}
denote
L({\mathcal{H}}\restriction\gamma,\cup_{\beta<\gamma}\beta^{\omega})
and
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\gamma}
denote
{\rm{HOD}}_{\cup_{\beta<\gamma}\beta^{\omega}}
.

Definition 1.16
.

Assume
{\sf{AD^{+}}}
and suppose
\gamma\leq\Theta
is an ordinal. Then the
Nairian model
at
\gamma
is
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\gamma}
.

Definition 1.17
.

Assume
{\sf{AD^{+}}}
and suppose
\gamma\leq\Theta
is an ordinal. Then

(1)

\gamma
is a
Nairian cardinal
if
N^{-}_{\gamma}\vDash\mathsf{ZF}
and
\gamma
is
{\mathrm{OD}}
-inaccessible,
13
13

13

That is,
\gamma
is a member of the Solovay sequence.
and

(2)

\gamma
is an
almost Nairian cardinal
if
N^{-}_{\gamma}\vDash\mathsf{ZF}
and if
\gamma
is not an
{\mathrm{OD}}
-inaccessible cardinal, then for any set
X\in N^{-}_{\gamma}
,
\mathscr{P}(X)\cap N_{\gamma}=\mathscr{P}(X)\cap N^{-}_{\gamma}
.

Assuming the short extender HOD analysis and
\mathrm{AD}^{+}
, Woodin showed that a
\mathrm{OD}
-inaccessible cardinal which is a limit of Woodin cardinals in HOD is a Nairian cardinal.
The third author has shown that the hypothesis of this theorem is equiconsistent with a Woodin cardinal which is a limit of Woodin cardinals. However, Corollary
10.17
implies that almost Nairian cardinals are strictly weaker than a Woodin cardinal that is a limit of Woodin cardinals. This is a key fact that leads to our calculations of upper bounds of failures of squares and failure of
K^{c}
constructions to converge. Because of this, we feel it is important to study almost Nairian cardinals as well as the Nairian cardinals.

Nairian models also arise naturally in hod mice. Suppose
{\mathcal{P}}\vDash\mathsf{ZFC}
is a hod premouse and
\kappa
is an inaccessible cardinal in
{\mathcal{P}}
. Let
{\mathcal{M}}_{\infty}
be the direct limit of all iterates
{\mathcal{Q}}
of
{\mathcal{P}}|\kappa
that are in
{\mathcal{P}}
and such that
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathcal{P}}|\kappa,{\mathcal{Q}}}
, the
{\mathcal{P}}
-to-
{\mathcal{Q}}
iteration tree, is of length
<\kappa
. Let
g\subseteq Coll(\omega,<\kappa)
be
{\mathcal{P}}
-generic. Then
N
is the Nairian model of
{\mathcal{P}}
at
\kappa
if, letting
\lambda={\sf{Ord}}\cap{\mathcal{M}}_{\infty}
,
N=L_{\lambda}({\mathcal{M}}_{\infty}^{\omega})
. Nairian models of hod mice also have interesting applications. For example,
[
11
]
uses them to reprove a theorem of Woodin that assuming proper class of Woodin limit of Woodin cardinals, the Chang Model is a model of AD.

Recently, Lukas Koschat, Sandra Müller, and the third author showed that if
\kappa
is a supercompact cardinal and there are class many supercompact cardinals, then the derived model at
\kappa
has cofinally many LSA pointclasses. The following conjecture is then a natural next step, though it may be much harder to prove.

Conjecture 1.18
.

Assume
\kappa
is a supercompact cardinal and there are class many supercompact cardinals. Let
M
be the derived model at
\kappa
. Then
M\vDash``\Theta
is a limit of Nairian cardinals.”

2.
Preliminaries

2.1.
Square principles

We recall the square principles introduced by Ronald Jensen
[
13
]
.

Definition 2.1

(
\square_{\kappa}
)
.

Let
\kappa
be an uncountable cardinal.
Then there is a sequence
\langle C_{\alpha}:\alpha<\kappa^{+}\rangle
such that for each
\alpha<\kappa^{+}
,

•

each
C_{\alpha}
is a closed cofinal subset of
\alpha
,

•

for each limit point
\beta
of
C_{\alpha}
,
C_{\beta}=C_{\alpha}\cap\beta
, and

•

the ordertype of each
C_{\alpha}
is at most
\kappa
.

Definition 2.2

(
\square(\gamma,\delta)
)
.

Let
\gamma
be an ordinal and
\delta
a cardinal.
Then there is a sequence
\langle\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}\mid\alpha<\gamma\rangle
such that

•

For each
\alpha<\gamma
,

–

0<|\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}|\leq\delta
,

–

each element of
\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}
is club in
\alpha
, and

–

for each member
C
of
\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}
, and each limit point
\beta
of
C
,

C\cap\beta\in\mathcal{C}_{\beta}.

•

There is no thread through the sequence, that is, there is no club
E\subseteq\gamma
such that
E\cap\alpha\in\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}
for every limit point
\alpha
of
E
.

A
\square(\gamma,\delta)
-
sequence
is a sequence
\langle C_{\alpha}:\alpha<\gamma\rangle
as in Definition
2.2
.
A
potential

\square(\gamma,\delta)
-sequence is a sequence
\langle C_{\alpha}:\alpha<\gamma\rangle
satisfying all but the last condition in Definition
2.2
.
An elementary argument shows that if the cofinality of
\gamma
is greater than
|\delta|^{+}
, then each potential
\square(\gamma,\delta)
-sequence has at most
|\delta|
many threads.
We note that if
\delta<\eta
, then
\square(\kappa,\delta)
implies
\square(\kappa,\eta)
.
Finally, the square principles are related.

\square(\gamma,1)
is the nonthreadability principle
\square(\gamma)
, and
for any cardinal
\kappa
,
\square_{\kappa}
implies
\square(\kappa^{+})
, as the ordertype condition in the definition of
\square_{\kappa}
ensures the nonthreadability of the sequence.

Todorčević showed
[
42
]
that if
\gamma
has cofinality at least
\omega_{2}
, then the restriction of the Proper Forcing Axiom (
\mathsf{PFA}
) to partial orders of cardinality
\gamma^{\omega}
implies
\square(\gamma,\omega_{1})
fails.
For
\gamma<\omega_{3}
, this fragment of
\mathsf{PFA}
follows from
\mathsf{MM}(\mathfrak{c})
, since
\mathsf{MM}(\mathfrak{c})
implies that
\mathfrak{c}=\aleph_{2}

[
10
]
.
Our only use of
\mathsf{MM}^{++}(\mathfrak{c})
will be to apply Todorčević’s Theorem
2.3
.

Theorem 2.3

(Todorčević)
.

If
\mathsf{MM}(\mathfrak{c})
holds, and
\gamma\in[\omega_{2},\omega_{3})
has cofinality
\omega_{2}
, then
\square(\gamma,\omega_{1})
fails.

Theorem
2.4
is one formulation of the main theorem of the first part of this paper (see Theorem
2.9
).

Theorem 2.4
.

The consistency of
\mathsf{ZFC}
plus the existence of a Woodin limit of Woodin cardinals implies, for each
n\in\omega
, the consistency of
\mathsf{ZFC}
+
\aleph_{2}^{\omega}=\aleph_{2}
+
\forall m\in(n\setminus 2)(\neg\square(\omega_{m},\omega))
.

It should be possible, given Todorčević’s theorem, to replace each instance of
\neg\square(\omega_{n},\omega)
with
\neg\square(\omega_{n},\omega_{1})
.

2.2.
The Nairian Model at
\lambda
and
\Join^{\xi}_{\lambda}

If
A\subseteq\omega^{\omega}
, then
w(A)
denotes the Wadge rank of
A
.
For any ordinal
\alpha
,
\Delta_{\alpha}
denotes the set of sets of reals of Wadge rank less than
\alpha
.

We will work with models of
\mathsf{AD}^{+}
14
14

14

An ostensible extension of the Axiom of Determinacy due to Woodin, see
[
16
]
.
in which some ordinal
\lambda
satisfies the following principle for all
n\in\omega
.

Definition 2.5

(
\Join^{n}_{\lambda}
)
.

Let
\kappa=\Theta^{{\mathrm{N}}_{\lambda}}
.
Then
\Join^{n}_{\lambda}
is the statement that

(1)

the order type of the set
C=\{\tau\in[\kappa,\lambda]:{\mathrm{N}}_{\lambda}\vDash``\tau
is a cardinal
"\}
is at least
n+1
,

(2)

\lambda<\Theta
and
\lambda\in C
,

(3)

\kappa
is a regular member of the Solovay sequence,

(4)

letting
\langle\kappa_{i}:i\leq n\rangle
be the first
n+1
members of
C
enumerated in increasing order,
15
15

15

Thus,
\kappa_{0}=\kappa
.
for every
i\leq n
,
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}\vDash``\kappa_{i}
is a regular cardinal”,

(5)

{\mathrm{N}}_{\lambda}^{-}\cap\mathscr{P}({\mathbb{R}})={\mathrm{N}}_{\lambda}^{+}\cap\mathscr{P}({\mathbb{R}})=\Delta_{\kappa}
,

(6)

for all
i\leq n
,
\mathscr{P}(\kappa_{i}^{\omega})\cap{\mathrm{N}}_{\kappa_{i+1}}^{-}=\mathscr{P}(\kappa_{i}^{\omega})\cap{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}
, and

(7)

for all
i+1\leq n
,
{\rm cf}(\kappa_{i+1})\geq\kappa
.

In light of Proposition
2.6
,
\Join^{m}_{\lambda}
implies
\Join^{n}_{\kappa_{n}}
for all
n\leq m
in
\omega
. Since
\mathsf{AD}^{+}
implies that successor members of the Solovay sequence below
\Theta
have countable cofinality
[
16
, Theorem 13.12]
,
\mathsf{AD}^{+}
+
\Join_{n}^{\xi}
implies that
\kappa=\Theta^{{\mathrm{N}}_{\lambda}}
is a limit member of the Solovay sequence.
Woodin has shown that
\mathsf{AD}^{+}
implies that
\mathsf{AD}^{+}
holds in every inner model of
\mathsf{ZF}
containing
{\mathbb{R}}
and that
\mathsf{AD}_{\mathbb{R}}
holds if and only if the Solovay sequence has limit length (see
[
16
]
).
Thus
\Join^{0}_{\lambda}
implies
L(\Delta_{\kappa})\vDash\mathsf{AD}_{\mathbb{R}}
.

Let
\ddagger
stand for the theory
\sf{ZF}
+
V=L(\mathscr{P}({\mathbb{R}}))
+
\sf{AD}_{\mathbb{R}}
+ “
\Theta
is regular”, which, as we have just seen, is satisfied by
L(\Delta_{\kappa})
.
Results of Solovay
[
34
]
show that
\ddagger
implies
\mathsf{DC}
(the statement that every tree of height
\omega
without terminal nodes has a cofinal branch) and that the sharp of each set of reals exists.
By results of Becker and Woodin,
\mathsf{AD}_{\mathbb{R}}+\mathsf{DC}
implies that all subsets of
\omega^{\omega}
are Suslin, and thus that
\mathsf{AD}^{+}
holds (see
[
16
]
).

Proposition
2.6
shows that the third item in the definition of
\Join^{n}_{\lambda}
could equivalently be replaced with the statement that
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}\cap\mathscr{P}({\mathbb{R}})=\Delta_{\kappa}
.

Proposition 2.6

(
\mathsf{AD}_{\mathbb{R}}
+ “
\Theta
is regular”)
.

\mathscr{P}({\mathbb{R}})\subseteq{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\Theta}
.

Proof.

Since
\mathsf{AD}
holds, every ultrafilter on the set of finite subsets of any element of
\Theta
is ordinal definable from an element of
\Theta
.
There is then an
\mathrm{OD}
-wellordering
\langle\nu_{\alpha}:\alpha<\Theta\rangle
of these ultrafilters.
Let
T
be set of tuples
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta)
such that
\nu_{\beta}
projects to
\nu_{\alpha}
, and the corresponding factor map sends
\gamma
above
\delta
.

Fix
A\subseteq\omega^{\omega}
. Then (since
\mathsf{AD}_{\mathbb{R}}
holds and
\Theta
is regular)
A
is homogeneously Suslin,
as witnessed by some set of measures
\langle\mu_{s}:s\in\omega^{\mathord{<}\omega}\rangle
. Let
f\colon\omega^{\mathord{<}\omega}\to\Theta
be such that each
\mu_{s}
is
\nu_{f(s)}
. Then
A
is the set of
x\in\omega^{\omega}
for which there is no
g\colon\omega\to\Theta
such that, for all
n\in\omega
,

(f(x\restriction n),f(x\restriction(n+1)),g(n),g(n+1))\in T.

∎

In the first part of the paper, we will work with models of
\exists\lambda\Join^{n}_{\lambda}
for positive integers
n
.
Such models are given by the following theorem from the second part of this paper (see Theorem
8.1
).

Theorem 2.7
.

Suppose there exists a Woodin cardinal which is a limit of Woodin cardinals.
Then in a forcing extension, there is an inner model satisfying
\ddagger
+
\exists\lambda\forall n<\omega\Join^{n}_{\lambda}
.

2.3.
Variants of
\mathsf{DC}

The principle of Dependent Choice (
\mathsf{DC}
) can be varied by restricting the nodes of the tree to some set, or by considering trees of uncountable height.

Given a set
X
and a cardinal
\gamma
, let
\mathsf{DC}_{\gamma}\restriction X
denote the restriction of
\mathsf{DC}_{\gamma}
to binary relations on
X
. (We also call this
\mathsf{DC}_{\gamma}

for relations on

X
.)
It is easy to see that
X
is wellordered if and only if
\mathsf{DC}_{\gamma}\restriction X
hold for all ordinals
\gamma
.

Given a binary relation
R
on a set
X
and an ordinal
\delta
, say that
f\colon\delta\rightarrow X
is an
R
-
chain
(of length
\delta
) if
f(\alpha)Rf(\beta)
holds for all ordinals
\alpha<\beta
below
\delta
.
Given an ordinal
\eta
, say that
R
is

\eta
-
full
if for all
\alpha<\beta<\delta
, every
R
-chain of length
\alpha
has an extension to an
R
-chain of length
\beta
.
Then
\sf{DC}_{\gamma}
holds for an ordinal
\gamma
if for every ordinal
\eta\leq\gamma
and
\eta
-full every binary relation
R
, there is an
R
-chain
f\colon\eta\rightarrow X
.

\mathsf{DC}
is just
\mathsf{DC}_{\omega}
.

2.4.
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}

We will need the following facts about Woodin’s
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}
from
[
48
]
:

(1)

\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}
conditions are elements of
H(\aleph_{1})
, and the corresponding order is definable in
H(\aleph_{1})
.

(2)

\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}
is
\sigma
-closed.

(3)

Forcing with
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}
over a model of
\mathsf{AD}^{+}+\mathsf{DC}
preserves the property of having cofinality at least
\omega_{2}
(this follows from a combination of Theorems 3.45 and 9.32 of
[
48
]
, as outlined in Section
3
).

(4)

If
M\vDash\mathsf{ZF}+\mathsf{AD}^{+}
and
G\subseteq\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}^{M}
is an
M
-generic filter, then the following hold in
M[G]
:

(a)

2^{\aleph_{0}}=\aleph_{2}
;

(b)

\Theta^{M}=\omega_{3}
;

(c)

\mathscr{P}(\omega_{1})\subseteq L_{\omega_{2}}({\mathbb{R}})[G]
.

(5)

Forcing with
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}
over a model of
\ddagger

produces a model of
\mathsf{ZF}
+
\mathsf{DC}_{\aleph_{2}}
+
\mathsf{MM}^{++}(\mathfrak{c})
.

Forcing with
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}
over a model of
\mathsf{AD}_{\mathbb{R}}
cannot wellorder
\mathscr{P}({\mathbb{R}})
, since if
\tau
were a
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}
-name for such a wellorder then every element of
\mathscr{P}({\mathbb{R}})
would be ordinal definable from
\tau
and a real number, which would induce a failure of Uniformization.
16
16

16

As in
[
16
, Theorem 6.28]
, which is due to Kechris and Solovay.
(Uniformization is the statement that every subset of
\omega^{\omega}\times\omega^{\omega}
contains a function with the same domain; this follows easily from the existence of a wellordering of
\omega^{\omega}
, and also from
\mathsf{AD}_{\mathbb{R}}
.)

However,
\mathsf{DC}_{\aleph_{2}}+2^{\aleph_{0}}=\aleph_{2}
implies that
\mathscr{P}({\mathbb{R}})
may be wellordered by forcing with
\mathrm{Add}(\omega_{3},1)
.
17
17

17

For any ordinal
\gamma
,
\mathrm{Add}(\gamma,1)
is the partial order adding a generic subset of
\gamma
by initial segments.

Since (by
\mathsf{DC}_{\aleph_{2}}
)
\mathrm{Add}(\omega_{3},1)
does not add subsets of
\omega_{2}
,
\mathsf{MM}^{++}(\mathfrak{c})
is preserved by forcing with
\mathrm{Add}(\omega_{3},1)
in this context.
This gives Theorem
2.8
.

Theorem 2.8

(Woodin
[
48
, Theorem 9.39]
)
.

Forcing with

\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}*\mathrm{Add}(\omega_{3},1)

over a model of
\ddagger

produces a model of
\mathsf{ZFC}
+
\mathsf{MM}^{++}(\mathfrak{c})
.

It follows from Todorčević’s Theorem
2.3
that
\square(\omega_{2},\omega_{1})
fails in such a
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}
extension.

The results discussed in §
2.2
imply that assuming
\ddagger
+
\Join^{n}_{\lambda}
(for any
n\in\omega
), we have:

•

\mathsf{AD}_{\mathbb{R}}
+
V=L(\mathscr{P}({\mathbb{R}}))
+ “
\Theta
is regular,”

•

the sharp of each subset of
{\mathbb{R}}
exists, and

•

{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}

\vDash

\mathsf{AD}_{\mathbb{R}}
+ “
\Theta
is regular.”

In the first part of this paper we will be forcing over a model of the form
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}
satisfying
\Join^{n}_{\lambda}
, for some ordinal
\lambda
and an arbitrary positive integer
n
. In this case,
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}
is not a model of “
V=L(\mathscr{P}({\mathbb{R}}))
”, since, being closed under ordinal definability, it contains the sharp of its version of
\mathscr{P}({\mathbb{R}})
(i.e.,
\Delta_{\kappa}
).
So we cannot just cite Theorem
2.8
for our main result.
In order to force Choice over a model of the form
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}
, it suffices to wellorder
\lambda^{\omega}
(see the beginning of Part I).
To do this, we will force (over a
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}
extension of
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}
, writing
\kappa_{i}
for
(\kappa^{+i})^{{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}}
) with the iteration

\mathrm{Add}(\kappa_{0},1)*\cdots*\mathrm{Add}(\kappa_{n},1),

which we will write as
\oast_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Add}(\kappa_{i},1)
or
(\oast_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Add}(\kappa^{+i},1))^{{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G]}
, where
G\subseteq\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}
is
V
-generic, noting that the
\kappa_{i}
’s are preserved as cardinals in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G]
(as in item
(\ref{job4})
from the beginning of Section
3
).

Theorem
2.9
, the main theorem of Part 1, builds on
[
48
]
and
[
8
]
, especially an argument from the proof of
[
8
, Theorem 7.3]
.

Theorem 2.9
.

Suppose that
V\vDash\ddagger
,
n
is a positive integer and
\lambda
is an ordinal for which
\Join^{n}_{\lambda}
holds. Let
\kappa=\Theta^{{\mathrm{N}}_{\lambda}}
and
let
(G,H)
be a
V
-generic filter for the forcing iteration

(\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}*\oast_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Add}(\kappa^{+i},1))^{{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}}.

Then

{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G,H]\vDash\mathsf{ZFC}+\mathsf{MM}^{++}(\mathfrak{c})+\forall i\leq(n+1)\,\neg\square(\omega_{2+i},\omega).

Remark 2.10
.

By item (
6
) from the definition of
\Join^{n}_{\lambda}
(see also Remark
5.6
), the partial orders

(\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}*\oast_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Add}(\kappa^{+i},1))^{{\mathrm{N}}_{\lambda}}

and

(\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}*\oast_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Add}(\kappa^{+i},1))^{{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}}

are the same, from which it follows that the theorem implies the corresponding version with
{\mathrm{N}}_{\lambda}
in place of
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}
.

Part I
Forcing over Nairian models

Fix
\kappa
,
\lambda
,
G
,
H
and
K
as in the statement of Theorem
2.9
, and write
H
as
(H_{0},\ldots,H_{n})
. We let
H\upharpoonright i
denote
\emptyset
when
i=0
and
(H_{0},\ldots,H_{i-1})
otherwise. We let
\kappa_{i}
denote
(\kappa^{+i})^{{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}}
for each
i\leq n
.
In particular,
\kappa_{0}=\kappa
and
\kappa_{n}=\lambda
.

In
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}
, each set is a surjective image of
\lambda^{\omega}\times\gamma
, for some ordinal
\gamma
.
Hence
\mathsf{ZFC}
holds in any forcing extension of
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}
in which
\lambda^{\omega}\cap{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}
is wellordered.
Since
\lambda
has uncountable cofinality in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}
, the forcing

\mathrm{Add}(\lambda,1)^{{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G,H\restriction n]}
(i.e.,
\mathrm{Add}(\kappa_{n},1)
in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G,H\restriction n]
) adds such a wellordering.

3.
Threading coherent sequences

The material in this section is mostly adapted from
[
8
]
and reduces the proof of Theorem
2.9
to showing the following (see Theorem
3.3
and the remarks afterwards):

(1)

\oast_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Add}(\kappa^{+i},1)
is
\mathord{<}\omega_{2}
-directed closed in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G]
;

(2)

V[G]\vDash((\oast_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Add}(\kappa^{+i},1))^{{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G]})^{\omega_{1}}\subseteq{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G]
;

(3)

\forall i\leq n

{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G,H\upharpoonright i]\vDash\mathsf{DC}_{\aleph_{2+i}}
;

(4)

\forall i\leq n
,
(\kappa^{+i})^{{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}}=(\kappa^{+i})^{{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G,H]}

Item (
1
) above follows from the fact that
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G]\vDash\mathsf{DC}_{\aleph_{2}}
,
18
18

18

The case
i=0
follows from item (
3
).
which is shown in Lemma
5.4
.
Item (
2
) is Lemma
6.2
.
Item (
3
) is Lemma
7.3
.
Item (
4
) follows from item (
3
), which implies that, for each
i\leq n
, the forcing
\mathrm{Add}(\kappa_{i},1)
over
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G,H\restriction i]
doesn’t add bounded subsets of
\kappa_{i}
, and Lemma
5.7
, which shows that each
\mathrm{Add}(\kappa_{i},1)
preserves the regularity of
\kappa_{j}
for all
j\in(i,n]
.

To apply Todorčević’s Theorem
2.3
to show that
\square(\omega_{3},\omega),\ldots,\square(\omega_{3+n},\omega)
all fail in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G,H]
, we need to show that
\kappa_{1},\ldots,\kappa_{n}
from Theorem
2.9
have cofinality
\omega_{2}
in

V[G]
.
(Recall that they are less than
\Theta^{V}
, which is
\omega_{3}^{V[G]}
.)
This is a consequence of covering results by Woodin, which we briefly review.
We refer the reader to
[
48
, Definition 3.30]
for the definition of
A
-iterability
.
Given
X\prec H(\omega_{2})
,
M_{X}
denotes its transitive
collapse.

Theorem 3.1

(Woodin
[
48
, Theorem 3.45]
)
.

Suppose that
M
is a proper class inner model containing
{\mathbb{R}}
and satisfying
\mathsf{AD}+\mathsf{DC}
.
Suppose that for any
A\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{R}})\cap M
, the set

\{X\prec H(\omega_{2})\mid\mbox{\rm$X$ is countable, and $M_{X}$ is $A$-iterable}\}

is stationary. Let
X
in
V
be a bounded subset of
\Theta^{M}
of size
\omega_{1}
. Then there is a set

Y\in M
, of size
\aleph_{1}
in
M
, such that
X\subseteq Y
.

We apply Theorem
3.1
in the proof of Lemma
3.2
with
M
as a model of the form
L(A,{\mathbb{R}})
for some
A\subseteq\omega^{\omega}
, and the
V
of Theorem
3.1
as a
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}
extension of
M
.

Lemma 3.2
.

Suppose that
M
is a model of
\mathsf{ZF}+\mathsf{AD}^{+}
and
\gamma
is an ordinal of cofinality at least

\omega_{2}
in
M
.
Let
G_{0}\subset\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}
be an
M
-generic filter. Then
\gamma
has cofinality at least
\omega_{2}
in

M[G_{0}]
.

Proof.

Suppose first that
\gamma<\Theta^{M}
. Let
X
be a subset of
\gamma
of cardinality
\aleph_{1}
in
M[G_{0}]
,
and let
A\in\mathscr{P}(\omega^{\omega})\cap M
have Wadge rank at least
\gamma
. Since
|\gamma|\leq 2^{\aleph_{0}}
in
M[G_{0}]
and
\mathscr{P}(\omega_{1})^{M[G_{0}]}
is contained in
L(A,\mathbb{R})[G]
,

X
is in
L(A,\mathbb{R})[G]
. By
[
48
, Theorem 9.32]
, the hypotheses of Theorem
3.1
are satisfied with
L(A,{\mathbb{R}})
as
M
and
L(A,{\mathbb{R}})[G]
as
V
.
Applying Theorem
3.1
, we have that
X
is a subset of an element
of
L(A,\mathbb{R})
of cardinality
\aleph_{1}
in
L(A,\mathbb{R})
.

The lemma follows immediately from the previous paragraph for
\gamma
of cofinality less than
\Theta^{M}
in
M
.
If
\gamma\geq\Theta
is regular in
M
, there is no cofinal function from
\omega^{\omega}
to
\gamma
in
M
, so there is no such
function in
M[G_{0}]
, either.
The theorem then follows for arbitrary
\gamma
.
∎

A simpler proof of a weaker version of Lemma
3.2
assuming
\mathsf{AD}_{\mathbb{R}}
plus the regularity of
\Theta
(which suffices for the results of this paper) is given in
[
18
]
.

In conjunction with the facts mentioned at the beginning of this section, Theorem
3.3
, with
M_{1}
as
V
,
M_{0}
as
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}
,
\gamma
as any member of
\{\kappa_{0},\ldots,\kappa_{n}\}
and
\mathbb{Q}
as
(\oast_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Add}(\kappa^{+i},1))^{{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G]}
, completes the proof of Theorem
2.9
.
The theorem and its proof are from
[
8
]
, except that the specific partial order used in
[
8
]
has been replaced with a more general class of partial orders.

Theorem 3.3
.

Suppose that
M_{1}
is a model of
\ddagger
, and that for some set
X\in M_{1}
containing

\omega^{\omega}\cap M_{1}
,
M_{0}={\rm{HOD}}^{M_{1}}_{X}
. Suppose also that
\Theta^{M_{0}}<\Theta^{M_{1}}
and that
\gamma\in[\Theta^{M_{0}},\Theta^{M_{1}})
has cofinality at least
\omega_{2}
in
M_{1}
.
Let
G_{0}\subset\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}
be
M_{1}
-generic, and let
I\subset\mathbb{Q}
be

M_{1}[G_{0}]
-generic, for some partial order
\mathbb{Q}\in M_{0}[G_{0}]
which, in
M_{1}[G_{0}]
, is

<\omega_{2}
-directed closed and of cardinality at most
\mathfrak{c}
.
Then
\square(\gamma,\omega)
fails in
M_{0}[G_{0}][I]
.

Proof.

Suppose that
\tau
is a
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}*\dot{\mathbb{Q}}
-name in
M_{0}
for a

\square(\gamma,\omega)
-sequence. We may assume that the realization of
\tau
comes with
an indexing of each member of the sequence in order type at most
\omega
. In
M_{1}
,
\tau
is
ordinal definable from some
S\in X
.

By
[
48
, Theorems 9.35 and 9.39]
,
\mathsf{DC}_{\aleph_{2}}
and
\mathsf{MM}^{++}(\mathfrak{c})
hold in
M_{1}[G_{0}]
.
By Lemma
3.2
,

\gamma
has cofinality
\omega_{2}
in
M_{1}[G_{0}]
.
Forcing with
{<}\omega_{2}
-directed closed partial orders
of size at most
\mathfrak{c}
preserves both
\mathsf{MM}^{++}(\mathfrak{c})
(see
[
17
]
) and the cofinality of
\gamma
.
Thus
\mathsf{DC}_{\aleph_{1}}
and
\mathsf{MM}^{++}(\mathfrak{c})
hold in the
\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{G_{0}}
-extension of
M_{1}[G_{0}]
,
and in this extension, by Todorčević’s Theorem
2.3
, every potential
\square(\gamma,\omega)
-sequence is
threaded.

Let
\mathcal{C}=\langle\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}:\alpha<\gamma\rangle
be the realization of

\tau
in the
\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{G}
-extension of
M_{1}[G_{0}]
.
Since
\gamma
has cofinality
\omega_{2}
in this
extension (which satisfies
\mathsf{DC}_{\aleph_{1}}
),
\mathcal{C}
has at most
\omega
many
threads, since otherwise one could find a
\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}
in the sequence with uncountably
many members.
Therefore, some member of some
\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}
in the realization of
\tau

will be extended by a unique thread through the sequence, and since the realization of
\tau
indexes
each
\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}
in order type at most
\omega
, there is in
M_{1}
a name, ordinal
definable from
S
, for a thread through the realization of
\tau
.
This name is then a member of

M_{0}={\rm{HOD}}^{M_{1}}_{X}
.
∎

3.1.
Proof of Theorem
2.9

We now complete the proof of Theorem
2.9
, assuming the statements (
1
)-(
4
) from the beginning of this section. We apply Theorem
3.3
with
V
as
M_{1}
and
X
as
\lambda^{\omega}
. Then the model
M_{0}
from Theorem
3.3
is
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}
, and
\kappa=\Theta^{M_{0}}<\Theta^{M_{1}}
by the hypotheses of Theorem
2.9
. Let
G=G_{0}
be
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}
-generic over
V
, and let
\mathbb{Q}
be the partial order
\oast_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Add}(\kappa^{+i},1)
as defined in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G]
.
By item (1) from the beginning of this section,
\mathbb{Q}
is
\mathord{<}\omega_{2}
-directed closed in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}
. By item (2), every
\omega_{1}
-sequence from
\mathbb{Q}
in
V[G]
is in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G]
. It follows then that
\mathbb{Q}
is
\mathord{<}\omega_{2}
-directed closed in
V[G]
as well.
Letting
H
be
V[G]
-generic for
\mathbb{Q}
, it follows from Theorem
3.3
that, for each
\gamma\in[\kappa,\Theta^{V})
having cofinality at least
\omega_{2}
in
V
,
\square(\gamma,\omega)
fails in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G,H]
.
By Lemma
3.2
, and the assumption of
\Join^{n}_{\lambda}
, each of the ordinals
(\kappa^{+i})^{{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}}
for
i\leq n
satisfies these conditions on
\gamma
. By item (
4
), the cardinals
\kappa_{0},\ldots,\kappa_{n}
are the cardinals
\aleph_{3},\ldots,\aleph_{3+n}
in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G,H]
.
The remarks at the beginning of Part I also show that
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G,H]\vDash\mathsf{ZFC}
.

Since
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}\subseteq H(\aleph_{1})
, and
\kappa
is both regular and equal to
\Theta^{{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}}
,

(\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}*\mathrm{Add}(\kappa,1))^{L(\Delta_{\kappa})}=(\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}*\mathrm{Add}(\kappa,1))^{{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}}.

Since
{\rm{L}}(\Delta_{\kappa})\vDash\ddagger
, Theorem
2.8
implies that
L(\Delta_{\kappa})[G,H_{0}]
satisfies
\mathsf{MM}^{++}(\mathfrak{c})
and hence
\neg\square(\omega_{2},\omega)
.
Then
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G,H_{0}]
does as well, since these two models have the same
\mathscr{P}(\omega_{2})
.
Item (
3
) implies that
\mathsf{DC}_{\aleph_{3+i}}
holds in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G,H_{0},\ldots,H_{i}]
, for each
i<n
, so
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G,H_{0}]
and
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G,H]
have the same subsets of
\omega_{2}
.
Thus
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G,H]\vDash\mathsf{MM}^{++}(\mathfrak{c})
as well.
∎

4.
Proving
\mathsf{DC}_{\aleph_{m}}

One of our four remaining tasks is showing that

{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G,H\upharpoonright i]\vDash\mathsf{DC}_{\aleph_{2+i}}

for all
i\leq n
.
§
4.1
reduces each of these to the case of relations on
\lambda^{\omega}
.
In §
4.2
we
prove a lemma which, together with the results of §
5
, reduces this further to the case of relations on the sets
\kappa_{i}^{\omega}
for
i<n
.

That
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G]\vDash\mathsf{DC}_{\aleph_{2}}
is proved in Lemma
5.4
, using Lemma
4.3
. That each model
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G,H\restriction i]
(
0<i<n
) satisfies
\mathsf{DC}_{\omega_{2+i}}\restriction\lambda^{\omega}
is proved in §
7
.

4.1.
Reducing to
\mathsf{DC}_{\aleph_{m}}\restriction\lambda^{\omega}

As we have defined it,
\mathsf{DC}_{\aleph_{m}}
implies
\mathsf{DC}_{\aleph_{k}}
for all
k\leq m
. Since
\ddagger
implies
\mathsf{DC}
, Lemma
4.1
shows (in the case
m=0
) that
\mathsf{DC}
holds in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}
.

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
\mathbb{P}
be a partial order in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}
, and let
I\subseteq\mathbb{P}
be a
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}
-generic filter.
Let
m<\omega
be such that
\mathsf{DC}_{\aleph_{k}}
holds in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[I]
for all
k<m
.
Suppose that, in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[I]
, every
<\omega_{m}
-full tree on
\lambda^{\omega}
of height
\omega_{m}
has a cofinal branch.
Then
\mathsf{DC}_{\aleph_{m}}
holds in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[I]
.

Proof.

Fix a
<\omega_{m}
-full tree
T
in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[I]
. Fix an ordinal
\gamma
such that every node of
T
is the realization of a
\mathbb{P}
-name which is ordinal definable in
V_{\gamma}
from some element of
\lambda^{\omega}
.
Given
(n,\delta,x)\in\omega\times\gamma\times\lambda^{\omega}
, let

t_{n,\delta,x}

be the set defined in
V_{\gamma}
from
\delta
and
x
by the formula with Gödel number
n
.

Let
T^{\prime}
be the tree of sequences
\langle x_{\alpha}:\alpha<\beta\rangle
(for some
\beta<\omega_{m}
) for which there exists a sequence

\langle y_{\alpha}:\alpha<\beta\rangle

such that, for each
\eta<\beta
,

y_{\eta}=t_{n,\delta,x_{\eta},I},

where
(n,\delta)\in(\omega,\gamma)
is minimal such that
t_{n,\delta,x_{\eta}}
is a
\mathbb{P}
-name and

\langle y_{\alpha}:\alpha<\eta\rangle^{\frown}\langle t_{n,\delta,x_{\eta},I}\rangle\in T.

Then
T^{\prime}
is also
<\omega_{m}
-full, and an
\omega_{m}
-chain through
T^{\prime}
induces one through
T
.
∎

4.2.
Proving
\mathsf{DC}_{\aleph_{2}}|\lambda^{\omega}

The following reflection argument is useful for establishing forms of
\mathsf{DC}
in the models we consider.

Lemma 4.2

(
\mathsf{ZF}
)
.

Suppose that
\tau
and
\rho
are infinite cardinals such that
there is no cofinal function
f\colon(\rho^{\omega})^{\tau}\to\rho^{+}
.
If
\mathsf{DC}_{\tau^{+}}\restriction\rho^{\omega}
holds, then

\mathsf{DC}_{\tau^{+}}\restriction(\rho^{+})^{\omega}
also holds.

Proof.

Let
T
be a
\leq\tau
-full tree on
(\rho^{+})^{\omega}
of height
\tau^{+}
. The hypotheses imply that there is a
\gamma\in[\rho,\rho^{+})
such that every node of
T\cap(\gamma^{\omega})^{<\tau^{+}}
has a proper extension in
T\cap(\gamma^{\omega})^{<\tau^{+}}
. Let
b\colon\rho\to\gamma
be a bijection, and let
T_{b}
be the set of
\langle a_{\alpha}:\alpha<\beta\rangle\in(\rho^{\omega})^{\mathord{<}\tau^{+}}
for which
\langle b\circ a_{\alpha}:\alpha<\beta\rangle\in T
. Since
\mathsf{DC}_{\tau^{+}}\restriction\rho^{\omega}
holds, there is a cofinal branch
\langle a_{\alpha}:\alpha<\tau^{+}\rangle
through
T_{b}
. Then
\langle b\circ a_{\alpha}:\alpha<\tau^{+}\rangle
is a cofinal branch through
T
.
∎

Lemma
4.2
naturally enables induction arguments.
We now prove the base case for one such argument. Recall that
\Theta^{M}=\omega_{3}^{M[G]}
in the context of the lemma.

Lemma 4.3
.

Suppose that
M
is a model of
\mathsf{AD}^{+}
containing
{\mathbb{R}}
, and that
G\subseteq\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}
is
M
-generic. Then for all
\gamma<\Theta^{M}
,
M[G]\vDash\mathsf{DC}_{\infty}\restriction\gamma^{\omega}
. If in addition
M\vDash``\Theta
is regular”, then

M[G]\vDash\mathsf{DC}_{\aleph_{2}}\restriction\omega_{3}^{\omega}
.

Proof.

The first conclusion of the lemma follows from the fact that forcing with
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}
wellorders
{\mathbb{R}}
.
Suppose then that
M\vDash``\Theta
is regular”.
Then in
M[G]
we have

•

2^{\aleph_{0}}=\aleph_{2}
,

•

\Theta^{M}=\omega_{3}
is regular and

•

\aleph_{2}^{\aleph_{1}}=\aleph_{2}
,

from which it follows that there is no cofinal function in
M[G]
from
(\gamma^{\omega})^{\beta}
to
\Theta^{M}
, for any
\gamma<\Theta^{M}
and
\beta<\omega_{2}^{M}
. A reflection argument as in the proof of Lemma
4.2
then gives the second conclusion of the lemma.
∎

In §
5
we establish the first hypothesis of Lemma
4.2
in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G]
for
\tau=\omega_{1}
and
\rho=\kappa_{i}
, for all
i<n
.
In conjunction with the two lemmas in this section, this gives that
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G]\vDash\mathsf{DC}_{\aleph_{2}}
.

19
19

19

I don’t think we need the rest of this, but leaving it here for easy access if needed. To show that
\mathsf{DC}_{\aleph_{2}}\restriction\lambda^{\omega}
holds in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G]
, we show that the following statements hold in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G]
:

•

there is no cofinal map from
\omega_{2}
to
\lambda
;

•

there is no cofinal map from
(\gamma^{\omega})^{\beta}
to
\lambda
, for any
\gamma<\lambda
and
\beta<\omega_{2}
(it suffices to show this for
\gamma=\kappa
and
\beta=\omega_{1}
).

The first of these follows from Lemma
5.1
with
b
as
\omega_{2}\times\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}
. The second is shown in the proof of Lemma
5.4
, whose statement is just the desired statement that
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G]\vDash\mathsf{DC}_{\aleph_{2}}
. These two facts imply that for every cardinal
\delta\leq\aleph_{2}
and each
\delta
-full relation
R
on
\lambda^{\omega}
in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G]
, there exists a
\gamma<\lambda
such that
R\cap\gamma^{\omega}
is also
\delta
-full. Since
\lambda=\kappa^{+}
, it suffices then (once we have established the two facts above) to consider trees on
\kappa^{\omega}
.
To show that, in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G]
, every
\omega_{2}
-full tree on
\kappa^{\omega}
has a cofinal branch, we use the fact (which follows from standard
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}
arguments) that the following statements hold in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G]
:

•

there is no cofinal map from
\omega_{2}
to
\kappa
(because
\kappa=\Theta^{{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}}
is regular in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}
and
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}\subseteq H(\aleph_{1})
);

•

there is no cofinal map from
(\gamma^{\omega})^{\beta}
to
\kappa
, for any
\gamma<\kappa
and
\beta<\omega_{2}
(because
\kappa=\omega_{3}^{{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G]}
and
\aleph_{2}^{\aleph_{1}}=\aleph_{2}
).

These facts imply that it suffices to consider
\omega_{2}
-full trees on
\gamma^{\omega}
for any
\gamma<\kappa
.
Since each such
\gamma^{\omega}
is a surjective image of the wellordered set
\omega^{\omega}
in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G]
,

{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G]
satisfies the statement that each such tree has a cofinal branch.

5.
Strong regularity of
\lambda

As above, let
\kappa_{i}
denote
(\kappa^{+i})^{{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}}
.
A cardinal
\rho
is
strongly regular
in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}
if whenever
b
is in
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\rho}
and
f\colon b\to\rho
is in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}
the range of
f
is bounded in
\rho
, i.e. there is a
\gamma<\rho
such that
f[b]\subseteq\gamma
.
In this section we show that each of the cardinals
\kappa_{i}
,
i\leq n
, is strongly regular in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}
.
We then derive several consequences, including the fact that
\mathsf{DC}_{\aleph_{2}}
holds in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G]
.

Lemma 5.1
.

For each
i\leq n
,
\kappa_{i}
is strongly regular in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}
.

Proof.

We work in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}
and proceed by induction on
i
.
When
i=0
, the lemma follows from
the facts that
\Theta
is regular and each member of
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\Theta}
is a surjective image of the reals.

Fix
i<n
for which the lemma holds, and fix
b\in{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{k_{i+1}}
and
f\colon b\to\kappa_{i+1}
.
Let
\beta<\kappa_{i+1}
be such that
b\in{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\beta}
.
Since
\kappa_{i+1}=\kappa_{i}^{+}
, there exists a surjection
h\colon\kappa_{i}\to\beta
.
Let
B
be the set of
y\in\kappa_{i}^{\omega}
such that
b
has a member definable in
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\beta}
from

h\circ y
and
{\mathcal{H}}\restriction\beta
.
Then
B
induces a surjection
g\colon\kappa_{i}^{\omega}\to b
.

Since
\kappa_{i}
is strongly regular, the ordertype of
f[g[\alpha^{\omega}]]
is less than
\kappa_{i}
for each
\alpha<\kappa_{i}
.
Since
\kappa_{i+1}
is regular,
f[g[\alpha^{\omega}]]
is a bounded subset of
\kappa_{i+1}
, for each
\alpha<\kappa_{i}
.
Again applying the regularity of
\kappa_{i+1}
,
f[b]=f[g[\kappa_{i}^{\omega}]]
is bounded in
\kappa_{i+1}
.
∎

Since
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}
is in
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa}
, Lemma
5.1
has the following immediate consequence.
In particular, each
\kappa_{i}
,
i\leq n
, remains regular in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G]
.

Lemma 5.2
.

For each
i\leq n
, each
b\in{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{i}}[G]
and each function
f\colon b\to\kappa_{i}
in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G]
, the range of
f
is bounded in
\kappa_{i}
.

The following lemma will be used to prove that
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G]\vDash\mathsf{DC}_{\aleph_{2}}
.

Lemma 5.3
.

For all
i\leq n
and all
b\in{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{i}}
,
\mathscr{P}(b)\cap{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}\subseteq{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{i}}
.

Proof.

Again, we work in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}
.
When
i=0
, the lemma follows from Proposition
2.6
and the fact that each member of
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa}
is a surjective image of the reals in
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa}
.

Fixing
i<n
for which the lemma holds, we show that it holds for
i+1
.
Fix
b\in{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{i+1}}
and
\beta<\kappa_{i+1}
such that

b\in{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\beta}
.
Let

h\colon\kappa_{i}\to\beta
be a surjection in
{\mathrm{N}}_{\kappa_{i+1}}
.
Fix
a\in\mathscr{P}(b)\cap{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}
, and let
B_{a}
be the set of
(x,n)\in\kappa^{\omega}\times\omega
such that some member of
a
is definable over
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\beta}
from
h\circ x
and
{\mathcal{H}}\restriction\beta
via the formula with Gödel number
n
.
Then
B_{a}\in{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}
.
Since
\mathscr{P}(\kappa_{i}^{\omega})\cap{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}\subseteq{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{i+1}}
, we have
B_{a}\in{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{i+1}}
, so
a\in{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{i+1}}
.
∎

We have enough in place to prove
\mathsf{DC}_{\aleph_{2}}
.

Lemma 5.4
.

{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G]\vDash\mathsf{DC}_{\aleph_{2}}
.

Proof.

By Lemmas
4.2
and
4.3
, it suffices to shows that cofinal map from
\kappa_{i}^{\omega_{1}}
to
\kappa_{i+1}
in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G]
for any
i<n
.
Fix such an
i
and an
f\colon\kappa_{i}^{\omega_{1}}\to\kappa_{i+1}
in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G]
.
By Lemma
5.2
, each element of
\kappa_{i}^{\omega_{1}}
in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G]
has range bounded below
\kappa_{i+1}
.
By Lemma
5.2
applied to
\kappa_{i+1}
, it suffices to show that for each
\gamma<\kappa_{i}
,
f[\gamma^{\omega_{1}}]
is bounded below
\kappa_{i+1}
.
Fix such a
\gamma
.
Each element of
\gamma^{\omega_{1}}
is the realization of a
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}
name which coded by a subset of
\gamma\times\omega_{1}\times\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}
, and which is therefore in
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{i}}
, by Lemma
5.3
.
It follows that
\gamma^{\omega_{1}}\cap{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}
is an element of
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{i+1}}
.
One more application of Lemma
5.2
then completes the proof.
∎

The rest of this section is devoted to proving Lemma
5.7
, which, as discussed at the beginning of §
3
, proves item (
4
) with the help of item (
3
).

Remark 5.5
.

By Proposition
2.6
, each set of reals in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}
is in
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa}
.
Since
\kappa
is regular, each condition in
(\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}*\mathrm{Add}(\kappa,1))^{{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}}
is coded by a set of reals and is therefore in
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa}
.
It follows that
(\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}*\mathrm{Add}(\kappa,1))^{{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}}
is in
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{1}}
.

Remark 5.6
.

Since
\kappa_{1}
is regular, each
(\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}*\mathrm{Add}(\kappa,1))^{{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}}
-name for a condition in
\mathrm{Add}(\kappa_{1},1)
is a subset of
(\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}*\mathrm{Add}(\kappa,1))^{{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}}\times\gamma
for some
\gamma<\kappa_{1}
, and is therefore in
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{1}}
, by Lemma
5.3
.
Thus each condition in

(\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}*\oast_{i\leq 1}\mathrm{Add}(\kappa^{+i},1))^{{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}}
is in
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{1}}
, and therefore
(\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}*\oast_{i\leq 1}\mathrm{Add}(\kappa^{+i},1))^{{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}}
is in
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{2}}
.
The same arguments show that, for each
m\leq n
, each condition in

(\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}*\oast_{i\leq m}\mathrm{Add}(\kappa^{+i},1))^{{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}}

is in
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{m}}
, and that, for each
m<n
,
(\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}*\oast_{i\leq m}\mathrm{Add}(\kappa^{+i},1))^{{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}}
is in
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{m+1}}
.

Remarks
5.5
and
5.6
give the following lemma, which implies that each
\kappa_{m+1}
remains regular in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G,H_{0},\ldots,H_{m}]
.

Lemma 5.7
.

The following hold for all
m<p\leq n
and all

b\in{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{p}}[G,H_{0},\ldots,H_{m}]
.

(1)

\mathscr{P}(b)\cap{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}\subseteq{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{p}}
;

(2)

For each

f\colon b\to\kappa_{p}

in
{\mathrm{N}}_{\lambda}^{+}[G,H_{0},\ldots,H_{m}]
, there exists a
\gamma<\kappa_{p}
such that
f[b]\subseteq\gamma
.

Proof.

Fix a name
\tau
in
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{p}}
such that
b
is the realization of
\tau
. For part (1), every subset of
b
in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}
is the realization of a name which can be coded by a subset of

\mathrm{tc}(\{\tau\})\times(\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}*\oast_{i\leq m}\mathrm{Add}(\kappa^{+i},1)).

Part (1) then follows from Lemma
5.2
and Remark
5.6
. Part (2) is similar, using Lemma
5.1
.
∎

6.
\omega_{1}
-closure in
V[G]

In this section we show that, in
V[G]
,
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G]
is closed under
\lambda
-sequences from

(\oast_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Add}(\kappa^{+i},1))^{{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G]}.

This is Lemma
6.2
below, which follows from Lemma
6.1
.

Lemma 6.1
.

In
V[G]
, for each
i\leq n
and
b\in{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{i}}[G]
,

b^{\omega_{1}}\in{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{i}}[G]
.

Proof.

We prove the lemma first in the case where
b\in{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{0}}[G]
, and then show that if it holds for
b\in{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{i}}[G]
, for some
i<n
, then it holds for
b\in{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{i+1}}
.

If
b\in{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{0}}[G]
, then there exists a
\gamma<\kappa_{0}
such that
b
and all of its members are in
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\gamma}[G]
. Since
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\gamma}
is a surjective image of
{\mathbb{R}}
in
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{0}}
, and
({\mathbb{R}}^{\omega_{1}})^{V[G]}\subseteq{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\gamma}[G]
(by item (
4c
) from the list at the beginning of Section
2.4
), it follows that
(b^{\omega_{1}})^{V[G]}\subseteq{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{0}}[G]
.

Fix
i<n
for which the lemma holds.
Since
\kappa_{i}<\Theta
,
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{i+1}}
is a surjective image of
\omega^{\omega}
in
V
.
Let
U\subseteq\omega^{\omega}
be such that
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{i+1}}
is a surjective image of
\omega^{\omega}
in
L(U,{\mathbb{R}})
.
Since
\omega^{\omega}
is wellordered in
L(U,{\mathbb{R}})[G]
, there exists in
L(U,{\mathbb{R}})[G]
a function picking for each

x\in{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{i+1}}[G]
a
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}
-name
\tau_{x}\in{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{i+1}}
such that
\tau_{x,G}=x
.
Since
\mathscr{P}(\omega_{1})\cap V[G]\subseteq L({\mathbb{R}})[G]
,

({\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{i+1}}[G])^{\omega_{1}}\cap V[G]\subseteq L(U,{\mathbb{R}})[G].

We work in
L(U,{\mathbb{R}})[G]
, which satisfies Choice.
Fix
b\in{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{i+1}}[G]
, and let
\beta<\kappa_{i+1}
be
such that
\Delta_{\kappa},\tau_{b}\in{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\beta}
.
It follows that every member of
b
is the realization of a name in
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\beta}
.
We show first that
b^{\omega_{1}}\subseteq{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{i+1}}[G]
.

Fix
f\in b^{\omega_{1}}
. Since Choice holds, there is an

h_{f}\in({\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\beta})^{\omega_{1}}

such that, for every
\alpha<\omega_{1}
,

h_{f}(\alpha)
is a
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}
-name in
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\beta}
such that
h_{f}(\alpha)_{G}=f(\alpha)
.
Fix a function
c_{f}\colon\omega_{1}\to\omega
and a sequence
\langle B_{\alpha}:\alpha<\omega_{1}\rangle
such that

(1.1)

each

B_{\alpha}
is a nonempty subset of
\beta^{\omega}
, and

(1.2)

each
h_{f}(\alpha)
is defined in

{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\beta}
from
{\mathcal{H}}\restriction\beta
and each member of the corresponding
B_{\alpha}
,
via the formula with Gödel number
c_{f}(\alpha)
.

Since
\mathscr{P}(\omega_{1})\subseteq L_{\omega_{2}}({\mathbb{R}})[G]
,
c_{f}\in{\mathrm{N}}_{\kappa_{i+1}}[G]
.

Let
h\colon\kappa_{i}\to\beta
be a surjection in
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{i+1}}

(which exists by Lemma
5.3
), and, for each
\alpha<\omega_{1}
, let

B^{\prime}_{\alpha}=\{x\in\kappa_{i}^{\omega}:h\circ x\in B_{\alpha}\}.

As there is no cofinal function from
\omega_{1}
to
\kappa_{i}
in

{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G]
, there is a
\gamma<\kappa_{i}
such that
B^{\prime}_{\alpha}\cap\gamma^{\omega}
is nonempty
for each
\alpha<\omega_{1}
. Since
\gamma^{\omega_{1}}\subseteq{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{i}}
by the induction hypothesis,
there is a sequence
\langle x_{\alpha}:\alpha<\omega_{1}\rangle\in(\gamma^{\omega})^{\omega_{1}}\cap{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{1}}[G]
such that each
x_{\alpha}
is in the corresponding
B^{\prime}_{\alpha}
. The sequence
\langle h\circ x_{\alpha}:\alpha<\omega_{1}\rangle
and the function
c_{f}
are both then in
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{i+1}}
. Along with
G
and
\beta
they can be used to define
f

in
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{i+1}}
.

Suppose now that
b^{\omega_{1}}\not\subseteq{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\alpha}[G]
for any
\alpha<\kappa_{i+1}
. We then have a function
g\colon b^{\omega_{1}}\to\kappa_{i+1}
that is unbounded in
\kappa_{i+1}
with

g\in{\mathrm{N}}_{\kappa_{i+1}}[G]
.
Using
\beta
and
h
as above, we can associate to each
f\in b^{\omega_{1}}
the set of pairs
(c,x)\in\omega^{\omega_{1}}\times\kappa_{i}^{\omega}
for which each value
f(\alpha)
is defined in
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\beta}
from
{\mathcal{H}}\restriction\beta
and
h\circ x
via the formula with Gödel number
c(\alpha)
. This induces a cofinal map from
\mathscr{P}(\omega_{1})\times\kappa_{i}^{\omega}
to
\kappa_{i+1}
. Since each member of
\kappa_{i}^{\omega}
has range bounded below
\kappa_{i}
(by the countable closure of
\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{max}}
), and since there is no cofinal function from
\kappa_{i}
to
\kappa_{i+1}
in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\kappa_{i+1}}[G]
by Lemma
5.2
, this induces a cofinal function from
\mathscr{P}(\omega_{1})\times\gamma^{\omega}
to
\kappa_{i+1}
in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\kappa_{i+1}}
, for some
\gamma<\kappa_{i}
.
By the induction hypothesis,
\mathscr{P}(\omega_{1})\times\gamma^{\omega}
is in
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{i}}[G]
, contradicting Lemma
5.2
.
∎

Lemma 6.2
.

V[G]\vDash((\oast_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Add}(\kappa^{+i},1))^{{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G]})^{\omega_{1}}\subseteq{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G]

Proof.

As noted in Remark
5.6
, each element of
(\oast_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Add}(\kappa^{+i},1))^{{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G]}

is in
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\lambda}[G]
.
Since
\mathop{\rm cof}\nolimits(\lambda)=\omega_{2}
in
V[G]
, every element of

((\oast_{i\leq n}\mathrm{Add}(\kappa^{+i},1))^{{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G]})^{\omega_{1}}

in
V[G]
has range contained in some element of
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\lambda}[G]
. The lemma then follows from Lemma
6.1
.
∎

7.
\sf{DC}_{\aleph_{2+i}}
in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G,H\restriction i]

Lemma
7.3
is the third item from the beginning of §
3
, and completes the proof of Theorem
2.9
.
We will fix an
i\leq n-1
for which the lemma is assumed to hold, and prove that it holds also for
i+1
.
The case
i=0
is slightly different from the others, so we do it separately in Lemma
5.4
.
The following lemma is needed for the case
i=0
.

Lemma 7.1
.

For each
j\leq n-1
and
\rho<\kappa
there are stationarily many
\eta<\kappa_{j+1}
such that, in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G,H_{0}]
,

{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\eta}[G,H_{0}]^{\rho}\subseteq{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\eta}[G,H_{0}].

Proof.

We work in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G,H_{0}]
.
Let (*) be the statement that, for all
\alpha<\beta<\kappa_{j+1}
, if there exists a surjection
s\colon\kappa_{j}\to\alpha
in
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\beta}
, then

{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\alpha}[G,H_{0}]^{\rho}\subseteq{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\beta}[G,H_{0}].

Then (*) implies that
\eta
is as desired if it has cofinality greater than
\rho
and has the property that for each
\alpha<\eta
there is a
\beta<\eta
such that
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\beta}
contains a surjection from
\kappa_{j}
to
\alpha
.
Since
\kappa_{j+1}
is regular by Lemma
5.7
, there are stationarily many such
\eta
.

Formally we prove (*) by induction on
j
. However, since the arguments for the base case and induction step are almost the same, we give them both at the same time and make explicit the differences at the end.

Suppose that
j\leq n-1
is given and (*), and therefore the lemma,
holds for all
k<j
.
Fix
\alpha<\beta
as given in (*), and let
s\colon\kappa\to\alpha
be a surjection in
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\beta}
.
Fix
f\colon\rho\to{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\alpha}[G,H_{0}]
.
For each
\gamma<\rho
, let
B_{\gamma}
be the set of
x\in\alpha^{\omega}
such that

f(\gamma)
is defined in
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\alpha}[G,H_{0}]
from
{\mathcal{H}}\restriction\alpha
,
G
,
H_{0}
and
x
via the formula with Gödel code
x(0)
.
Since
\mathop{\rm cof}\nolimits(\kappa_{j})>\rho
, there is a
\delta<\kappa_{j}
such that for all
\gamma<\rho
,

B^{\prime}_{\gamma}=\{y\in\delta^{\omega}:s\circ y\in B_{\gamma}\}

is nonempty.
In the case
j=0
, the sequence
\langle B^{\prime}_{\gamma}:\gamma<\rho\rangle
is coded by a set of reals in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G,H_{0}]
, so it is in
L(\Delta_{\kappa})[G]
. It follows that
f\in{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\beta}[G,H_{0}]
. In the case
j>0
,
\delta^{\omega}
is an element of
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{j-1}}
by the definition of
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{j-1}}
, so
\langle B^{\prime}_{\gamma}:\gamma<\rho\rangle
is an element of
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\xi}[G,H_{0}]
, for some
\xi<\kappa_{j-1}
, by the induction hypothesis. In either case,

\langle B_{\gamma}:\gamma<\rho\rangle
is in
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\beta}
, so
f
is as well.
∎

Lemma 7.2
.

{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G,H_{0}]\vDash\sf{DC}_{\aleph_{3}}
.

Proof.

The model
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G,H_{0}]
contains a wellordering of

\kappa^{\omega}
, so it satisfies
\mathsf{DC}_{\infty}\restriction\kappa^{\omega}
.
By Lemma
4.2
then, it suffices to show that, for each
j\leq n-1
, there is no cofinal function from
(\kappa_{j}^{\omega})^{\omega_{2}}
to
\kappa_{j+1}
in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G,H_{0}]
. For each such
j
, Lemma
7.1
implies that
(\kappa_{j}^{\omega})^{\omega_{2}}
is an element of
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\kappa_{j+1}}
. Part (2) of Lemma
5.7
implies that there is no cofinal function from
(\kappa_{j}^{\omega})^{\omega_{2}}
to
\kappa_{j+1}
, as desired.
∎

Lemma 7.3
.

For each
i\leq n
,
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G,H\restriction i]\vDash\sf{DC}_{\aleph_{2+i}}
.

Proof.

The cases
i=0
and
i=1
have been proved in Lemmas
5.4
and
7.2
, so we may assume that
i>1
, in which case
\aleph_{2+i-1}=\kappa_{i-2}
. We work in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}[G,H\restriction i]
. The set

\kappa_{i-1}^{\omega}
is wellordered, so
\mathsf{DC}_{\infty}\restriction\kappa_{i-1}^{\omega}
holds.
By Lemma
4.2
, it suffices to show that, for each
j
with
i\leq j\leq n-1
, there is no cofinal function from
(\kappa_{j}^{\omega})^{\kappa_{i-2}}
to
\kappa_{j+1}
. Of course,
(\kappa_{j}^{\omega})^{\kappa_{i-2}}
can equivalently be replaced with
\kappa_{j}^{\kappa_{i-2}}
. Again,

\kappa_{i-2}^{\kappa_{i-2}}
is wellorded by
H_{i-1}
in ordertype
\kappa_{i}
, and Lemma
5.7
implies that there is no cofinal function from
\kappa_{i}
to
\kappa_{j+1}
.
Now, for each
k
in the interval
[i-2,j-i]
, the regularity of
\kappa_{k+1}
and
\kappa_{j+1}
implies that if there is a cofinal function from
\kappa_{k+1}^{\kappa_{i-2}}
to
\kappa_{j+1}
, then there is a cofinal function from
\kappa_{k}^{\kappa_{i-2}}
to
\kappa_{j+1}
. Since there is no such function in the case
k=i-2
, we are done.
∎

Part II
Constructing Nairian models

The Main Theorem on Nairian Models

Theorem

is the main theorem of this part of the paper.

Theorem 8.1
.

Suppose
{\mathcal{V}}\vDash{\sf{ZFC}}
is a hod premouse

8.1.1.
Fine structure and iteration trees.

Our mice and hod mice use Jensen’s indexing scheme.
Given an extender
E
over a transitive model
M
, we let

(1)

\pi^{M}_{E}:M\rightarrow Ult(M,E)
be the ultrapower embedding,
24
24

24

We will often omit superscript
M
.

(2)

{\rm crit}(E)
be the critical point of
E
and
{\rm im}(E)=\pi_{E}^{M}({\rm crit}(E))
,
25
25

25

{\rm im}(E)
depends only on
E
. Indeed,
E
determines
\mathscr{P}({\rm crit}(E))\cap M
and if
\mathscr{P}({\rm crit}(E))\cap M=\mathscr{P}({\rm crit}(E))\cap N
then
\pi_{E}^{M}({\rm crit}(E))=\pi_{E}^{N}({\rm crit}(E))
.

(3)

E
be indexed at
{\rm lh}(E)=_{def}\pi_{E}(({\rm crit}(E)^{+}))
, and

(4)

{\rm gen}(E)
be the natural length of
E
, i.e. the supremum of the ordinals of the form
\nu+1
, where
\nu
is a generator of
E
.
26
26

26

See
[
40
, Chapter 2]
.

Our hod mice have only short extenders, meaning that
Ult(M,E)=\{\pi_{E}^{M}(f)(a):f\in M,f:[{\rm crit}(E)]^{\left|a\right|}\rightarrow M,a\in[{\rm im}(E)]^{<\omega}\}
.

We follow a convention introduced in
[
3
]
and used in
[
40
]
: A hod premouse
{\mathcal{M}}
is a pair
({\mathcal{M}}^{\prime},k({\mathcal{M}}))
, where
{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}
is what one usually calls a hod premouse, i.e.,
{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}=J_{\alpha}^{\vec{E},S^{\mathcal{M}}}
, and
k({\mathcal{M}})\leq\omega

is such that

{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}
is
k({\mathcal{M}})
-sound.
We will abuse notation and write
{\mathcal{M}}
for
{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}
as well.

\alpha({\mathcal{M}})
stands for
\alpha
, and

\lfloor{\mathcal{M}}\rfloor
denotes the universe of
{\mathcal{M}}
.

Let
{\mathcal{M}}
be a premouse and
\beta\leq{\sf{Ord}}\cap{\mathcal{M}}
.
We write
{\mathcal{M}}|\beta
for
(J_{\beta}^{\vec{E}^{\mathcal{M}}},\vec{E}^{\mathcal{M}}\restriction\beta,\in)
. We write
{\mathcal{M}}||\beta
for
(J_{\beta}^{\vec{E}^{\mathcal{M}}},\vec{E}^{\mathcal{M}}\restriction\beta,E_{\beta},\in)
. We write
l({\mathcal{M}})=(\alpha({\mathcal{M}}),k({\mathcal{M}}))
. For
(\beta,m)\leq l({\mathcal{M}})
, we write
{\mathcal{M}}|(\beta,m)
for
({\mathcal{M}}|\beta,m)
and
{\mathcal{M}}||(\beta,m)
for
({\mathcal{M}}||\beta,m)
. Here we are following the notation of
[
14
]
and
[
29
]
, which was later changed in
[
40
]
.

We review some concepts from
[
40
, Chapter 2]
.
Suppose that
{\mathcal{M}}
is a premouse and
n=k({\mathcal{M}})
. We say
E
is an
{\mathcal{M}}
-
extender
if
\mathscr{P}({\rm crit}(E))^{\mathcal{M}}
is measured by
E
and
{\rm crit}(E)<\rho_{n}({\mathcal{M}})
. Given an
{\mathcal{M}}
-extender
E
with
{\rm crit}(E)<\rho_{n}({\mathcal{M}})
, we set
Ult({\mathcal{M}},E)
to be the decoding of
Ult_{0}({\mathcal{M}}^{n},E)
, where
{\mathcal{M}}^{n}
is the
n
th reduct of
{\mathcal{M}}
. We also set
k(Ult({\mathcal{M}},E))=k({\mathcal{M}})
(provided the resulting ultrapower is
k({\mathcal{M}})
-sound).

Suppose next that
E
is an extender such that for some
(\beta,m)\leq_{lex}l({\mathcal{M}})
,
E
is an
{\mathcal{M}}||(\beta,m)
-extender. Letting
(\beta,m)\leq l({\mathcal{M}})
be largest such that
E
is an
{\mathcal{M}}||(\beta,m)
-extender, we let
Ult({\mathcal{M}},E)
be the decoding of
Ult_{0}(({\mathcal{M}}||\beta)^{m},E)
and
k(Ult({\mathcal{M}},E))=m
. If
(\beta,m)<l({\mathcal{M}})
, we say that
E
causes a
drop
. We let
\pi_{E}^{{\mathcal{M}}}:{\mathcal{M}}||\beta\rightarrow Ult({\mathcal{M}},E)
.

Given a premouse
{\mathcal{M}}
and ordinal
\lambda
, we write
\lambda\in{\rm dom}(\vec{E})^{\mathcal{M}}
to indicate that there is an extender
E
on the extender sequence of
{\mathcal{M}}
such that
{\rm lh}(E)=\lambda
.
We may also let
E^{\mathcal{M}}_{\lambda}
be the extender on the sequence of
{\mathcal{M}}
such that
{\rm lh}(E_{\lambda})=\lambda
.
If
P
is a property, then we say
E\in\vec{E}^{\mathcal{M}}
is the least that has property
P
if
{\rm lh}(E)
is the least ordinal
\alpha
such that there is
F\in\vec{E}^{\mathcal{M}}
with the property that
{\rm lh}(F)=\alpha
and
F
has the property
P
.

Our iteration trees are normal, i.e. the lengths of the extenders increase and each extender is applied to the earliest model it can be applied to (see
[
37
]
or
[
40
]
).
An iteration tree
{\mathcal{T}}
of length
\eta
on a fine structural model
{\mathcal{M}}
(a premouse, hod premouse, etc.) is a tuple

({\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha},\iota_{\alpha},\lambda_{\alpha},\xi_{\alpha},n_{\alpha},\pi_{\alpha,\beta},\mathcal{D},T:\alpha<\beta<\eta)

such that

(1)

T
is a tree order on
\eta
,

(2)

for
\alpha+1<\eta
,
T(\alpha+1)
is the predecessor of
\alpha+1
in the tree order given by
T
,

(3)

{\mathcal{M}}_{0}={\mathcal{M}}
,

(4)

for each
\alpha<\eta
,
\iota_{\alpha}\leq{\sf{Ord}}\cap{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}
,

(5)

if
\iota_{\alpha}\in{\rm dom}(\vec{E})^{{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}}
, then, setting
E_{\alpha}=E_{\iota_{\alpha}}^{{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}}
,
\lambda_{\alpha}={\rm im}(E_{\alpha})
and

{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha+1}=Ult({\mathcal{M}}_{T(\alpha+1)}||\xi_{\alpha},E_{\alpha}),

where
(\xi_{\alpha},n_{\alpha})\leq_{lex}l({\mathcal{M}}_{T(\alpha+1)})
is the largest such that
E_{\iota_{\alpha}}^{{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}}
is an extender over
{\mathcal{M}}_{T(\alpha+1)}||(\xi_{\alpha},n_{\alpha})
,

(6)

if
\iota_{\alpha}\not\in{\rm dom}(\vec{E}^{{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}})
, then
{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha+1}={\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}
27
27

27

We allow padding.
and
\lambda_{\alpha}=\iota_{\alpha}
,

(7)

for
\alpha+1<\beta+1<{\rm lh}({\mathcal{T}})
,
\iota_{\alpha}<\iota_{\beta}
and
\lambda_{\alpha}\leq\lambda_{\beta}
,

(8)

for
\alpha+1<{\rm lh}({\mathcal{T}})
,
\beta=T(\alpha+1)
if and only if
\beta
is the least
\gamma<{\rm lh}({\mathcal{T}})
such that
{\rm crit}(E_{\alpha})<\lambda_{\beta}
or
\alpha=\beta
,

(9)

\mathcal{D}
is the set of all those ordinals
\alpha+1
such that
(\xi_{\alpha},n_{\alpha})<_{lex}l({\mathcal{M}}_{T(\alpha+1)})
,

(10)

if
\alpha T\beta
and
\mathcal{D}\cap[\alpha,\beta)_{T}=\emptyset
, then
\pi_{\alpha,\beta}:{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}\rightarrow{\mathcal{M}}_{\beta}
is the iteration embedding,

(11)

if
\xi<\eta
is a limit ordinal, then there is

such that

and, setting

,

is the direct limit of
({\mathcal{M}}_{\beta},\pi_{\beta,\gamma}:\beta<\gamma\in b)
.

To relieve the letter
``T"
of its duty and use it elsewhere, we will write
{\mathcal{T}}
for
T
.
We will use superscript
{\mathcal{T}}
to denote objects in
{\mathcal{T}}
.
Given an iteration tree
{\mathcal{T}}
, we let

(12)

{\rm gen}({\mathcal{T}})=\sup\{\lambda_{\alpha}^{\mathcal{T}}:\alpha<{\rm lh}({\mathcal{T}})\}
, and

(13)

{\rm ext}_{\alpha}^{\mathcal{T}}={\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}^{\mathcal{T}}|\iota_{\alpha}^{\mathcal{T}}
.

Notation 8.3

(Concatenating iterations)
.

Suppose
{\mathcal{T}}
is an iteration tree on
{\mathcal{M}}
with last model
{\mathcal{N}}
, and
{\mathcal{U}}
is an iteration tree on
{\mathcal{N}}
.
If
{\mathcal{T}}
followed by
{\mathcal{U}}
is a normal iteration tree on
{\mathcal{M}}
, then we denote the composite iteration by
{\mathcal{T}}^{\frown}{\mathcal{U}}
.
On the other hand, if
{\mathcal{T}}
followed by
{\mathcal{U}}
is
not
a normal iteration tree on
{\mathcal{M}}
, then we write
{\mathcal{T}}\oplus{\mathcal{U}}
for this iteration.
If
E\in\vec{E}^{\mathcal{N}}
, then we write
{\mathcal{T}}^{n\frown}\{E\}
for the normal continuation of
{\mathcal{T}}
by
E
.

We will use some notation
from
[
25
]
.
For example, if

{\mathcal{T}}=({\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha},\iota_{\alpha},\lambda_{\alpha},\xi_{\alpha},n_{\alpha},\pi_{\alpha,\beta},\mathcal{D},T:\alpha<\beta<\eta)

is an iteration tree and
\gamma<{\rm lh}({\mathcal{T}})
, then we let

{\mathcal{T}}_{\geq\gamma}=({\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha},\iota_{\alpha},\lambda_{\alpha},\xi_{\alpha},n_{\alpha},\pi_{\alpha,\beta},\mathcal{D},T:\gamma\leq\alpha<\beta<\eta).

In general,
{\mathcal{T}}_{\geq\gamma}
may not be anything reasonable.
However, it is possible that
{\mathcal{T}}_{\geq\gamma}
can be represented as an iteration tree on
{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}^{\mathcal{T}}
after re-enumerating it.

Similarly, given
\gamma<\iota\leq{\rm lh}({\mathcal{T}})
, we will use
{\mathcal{T}}_{[\gamma,\iota)}
or other such notation to indicate the portion of
{\mathcal{T}}
that is between
\gamma
and
\iota
.

Continuing with
{\mathcal{T}}
, if
{\mathcal{N}}={\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}^{\mathcal{T}}
for some
\alpha\leq{\rm lh}({\mathcal{T}})
, then we may write
{\mathcal{T}}_{\geq{\mathcal{N}}}
instead of
{\mathcal{T}}_{\geq\alpha}
. In this case, we may also say that
{\mathcal{N}}
is a
node of

{\mathcal{T}}
. We may say that
{\mathcal{U}}
is a
fragment
of
{\mathcal{T}}
just in case for some
\gamma<\iota\leq{\rm lh}({\mathcal{T}})
,
{\mathcal{U}}={\mathcal{T}}_{[\gamma,\iota)}
.

Suppose now that
{\mathcal{T}}
is an iteration tree on
{\mathcal{M}}
and
w=(\nu,\mu)
. We then say that
{\mathcal{T}}
is
based
on
w
just in case
{\mathcal{T}}
is
strictly above

\nu
28
28

28

For every
\alpha<{\rm lh}({\mathcal{T}})
,
{\rm crit}(E_{\alpha}^{\mathcal{T}})>\nu
.
and
below

\mu
29
29

29

For every
\alpha<{\rm lh}({\mathcal{T}})
, either
[0,\alpha)_{\mathcal{T}}\cap D^{\mathcal{T}}\neq\emptyset
or
{\rm lh}(E_{\alpha}^{\mathcal{T}})<\pi_{0,\alpha}^{\mathcal{T}}(\mu)
.
.

8.1.2.
Hod premice.

We denote hod pairs using symbols

In the sequel, we will write

.

Remark 8.5
.

Suppose that
{\mathfrak{p}}
is a hod pair.
Comparison is what usually guarantees that
{\mathcal{M}}_{\infty}({\mathfrak{p}})
is defined.
Here, however, we require only a weaker form of comparison, saying that if
{\mathfrak{q}}
and
{\mathfrak{r}}
are two (countable) complete iterates of
{\mathfrak{p}}
, then there is a (countable) complete iterate
{\mathfrak{s}}
of
{\mathfrak{p}}
such that
{\mathfrak{s}}
is also a countable complete iterate of both
{\mathfrak{q}}
and
{\mathfrak{r}}
. Usually
{\mathcal{M}}_{\infty}
is defined in models of
{\sf{AD^{+}}}
, so by “countable iterate” we mean countable in the sense of the relevant determinacy model.
But in the statement of Theorem
8.1
,
{\mathcal{M}}_{\infty}
is defined using
<\eta
iterations.
First, notice that
\eta=\omega_{1}^{M}
, where
M
is as in Theorem
8.1
.
Second, notice that because
\Sigma
has full normalization, the system
\mathcal{F}
is indeed directed.
32
32

32

See Proposition
8.9
and also
[
6
, Theorem 6.4 and Lemma 6.5]
.

Suppose that
V\vDash{\sf{AD^{+}}}+{\sf{HPC}}+V=L(\mathscr{P}({\mathbb{R}}))
, and let
\theta_{\alpha}<\Theta
be a member of the Solovay sequence.
The results of
[
40
]
imply that there is a hod pair
{\mathfrak{p}}
such that
{\mathcal{M}}_{\infty}({\mathfrak{p}})\subseteq{\rm{HOD}}
with
V_{\theta_{\alpha}}^{\rm{HOD}}=\lfloor{\mathcal{M}}_{\infty}({\mathfrak{p}})|\theta_{\alpha}\rfloor
.

Terminology 8.6
.

Continuing with our hod pair
{\mathfrak{p}}
, let
{\mathfrak{q}}
be a complete iterate of
{\mathfrak{p}}
. We say
{\mathfrak{q}}

catches

\alpha
if
\alpha\in{\rm rge}(\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty})
. If
{\mathfrak{q}}
catches
\alpha
, then we let
\alpha_{\mathfrak{q}}
be the
\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}
-preimage of
\alpha
. Similarly, if
{\mathfrak{q}}
is a complete iterate of
{\mathfrak{p}}
,

, and
{\mathfrak{r}}
is a complete iterate of
{\mathfrak{q}}
, then we let
x_{\mathfrak{r}}=\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}}(x)
.

Suppose
{\mathcal{P}}
is a hod premouse.
We let
S^{\mathcal{P}}
be the strategy indexed on the sequence of
{\mathcal{P}}
.
Setting
\Sigma=S^{\mathcal{P}}
and fixing a
{\mathcal{P}}
-generic
g
, in many situations
\Sigma
can be extended to act on iterations in the generic extension
{\mathcal{P}}[g]
.
For example, if
\eta
is a regular cardinal of
{\mathcal{P}}
and
g
is a generic for a poset whose size is below a Woodin cardinal that is greater than
\eta
, then
\Sigma_{{\mathcal{P}}|\eta}
can be uniquely extended to an iteration strategy in
{\mathcal{P}}[g]
.
It is appropriate to call this strategy
\Sigma^{g}_{{\mathcal{P}}|\eta}
.
If
{\mathcal{P}}
is a hod premouse and
\eta
is an inaccessible limit of Woodin cardinals, then
S^{\mathcal{P}}_{{\mathcal{P}}|\eta}
can be uniquely extended to an
\omega_{1}
-strategy in
{\mathcal{P}}[g]
, where
g\subseteq Coll(\omega,<\eta)
is
{\mathcal{P}}
-generic.
We will suppress reference to the generic when it will not cause confusion.
See
[
40
, Chapter 11.1]
for more on interpreting iteration strategies in generic extensions.

8.1.3.
Least-extender-disagreement coiterations and minimal copies.

Suppose that
u=({\mathfrak{p}},{\mathfrak{q}})
is a pair of hod pairs. Then
({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{Y}})
is the
least-extender-disagreement coiteration
of
u
if
({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{Y}})
is obtained by iterating away the least disagreeing extenders.
33
33

33

As is done in the usual comparison argument for mice, see
[
37
]
.

Suppose next that
{\mathfrak{p}}
is a hod pair and
{\mathfrak{q}}
and
{\mathfrak{r}}
are complete iterates of
{\mathfrak{p}}
.
Then the least-extender-disagreement-coiteration of
({\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}})
is
successful
, i.e., the least-extender-disagreement-coiteration of
({\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}})
outputs a pair

such that
{\mathcal{X}}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}
have a common last model
{\mathfrak{s}}
. Moreover, for each

, exactly one of

and

is not
\emptyset
. Indeed, for each
\alpha\leq{\rm lh}({\mathcal{X}})
, letting

(1)

\xi
be the least such that
{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}^{\mathcal{X}}|\xi={\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}^{\mathcal{Y}}|\xi
,

(2)

{\mathcal{U}}
be the iteration tree on
{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathfrak{p}}

\Sigma^{\mathfrak{p}}
that is built by comparing

(3)

{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}
be the last model of
{\mathcal{U}}

is not

E_{\alpha}^{\mathcal{X}}\in\vec{E}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}
and
{\rm lh}(E_{\alpha}^{\mathcal{X}})=\xi
.

34

34

For more on the least-extender-disagreement-coiteration, see

, Theorem 6.4]
.

Suppose that

Definition 8.7

(Minimal copies)
.

Suppose that
{\mathcal{M}}
is a fine structural model and
F
is an
{\mathcal{M}}
-extender.
Set
{\mathcal{N}}=Ult({\mathcal{M}},F)
and
\pi=\pi_{F}^{\mathcal{M}}
. Suppose that
{\mathcal{T}}
is a normal iteration of
{\mathcal{M}}
above
{\rm crit}(F)
(i.e., all extenders used in
{\mathcal{T}}
have critical point
>{\rm crit}(F)
).
We can then (attempt to)
copy

{\mathcal{T}}
onto
{\mathcal{N}}
in a minimal fashion reminiscent of the full normalization procedure.
This minimal-copying procedure produces a normal iteration
{\mathcal{U}}
of
{\mathcal{N}}
with the following properties.

(1)

{\rm lh}({\mathcal{U}})={\rm lh}({\mathcal{T}})
,
U=T
., and for every
\alpha<{\rm lh}({\mathcal{T}})
,
{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}^{\mathcal{U}}=Ult({\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}^{\mathcal{T}},F)
.

(2)

For
\alpha<{\rm lh}({\mathcal{T}})
, let
\sigma_{\alpha}=\pi_{F}^{{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}^{\mathcal{T}}}
. Then
E_{\alpha}^{\mathcal{U}}=\bigcup_{x\in{\rm ext}^{\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}}\sigma_{\alpha+1}(x\cap E_{\alpha}^{\mathcal{T}})
.

We then say that
{\mathcal{U}}
is the
minimal
({\mathcal{M}},F)
-copy
or simply the
minimal
F
-copy
of
{\mathcal{T}}
, and
\sigma_{\alpha}

(\alpha<{\rm lh}({\mathcal{T}}))
are the
minimal copy maps
.
When
{\mathcal{M}}
is clear from context we will omit it from the notation.

The minimal
({\mathcal{M}},F)
-copy of
{\mathcal{T}}
may not exist, as a priori there is nothing that implies
E_{\alpha}^{\mathcal{U}}\in\vec{E}^{{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}^{\mathcal{U}}}
. However, minimal copies do come up in the full normalization process, and facts like
E_{\alpha}^{\mathcal{U}}\in\vec{E}^{{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}^{\mathcal{U}}}
can be established assuming our iteration strategy has nice properties (see
[
40
, Remark 6.16]
).
A key step towards full normalization is the fact that
Ult({\rm ext}^{\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha},F)\trianglelefteq Ult({\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}^{\mathcal{T}},F)
, which can be proved just like the fact that
E_{\alpha}^{\mathcal{U}}\in\vec{E}^{{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}^{\mathcal{U}}}
.
The following theorem is essentially the full normalization theorem (
[
38
, Theorem 1.4]
), and can be established using material from
[
40
, Chapter 6.1]
.
35
35

35

For more on full normalization, see
[
31
, Definition 3.7]
and
[
33
]
.

Then

40

40

It follows from the results of

that
\Sigma_{{\mathcal{M}}|\nu}

Proof.

Set
{\mathcal{Q}}={\mathcal{M}}^{\mathcal{T}}_{\beta}
and
{\mathcal{R}}={\mathcal{M}}^{\mathcal{T}}_{\alpha}
.
Let
{\mathcal{U}}
be a normal iteration tree of limit length that is according to both
\Sigma_{{\mathcal{Q}}}
and
\Sigma_{{\mathcal{R}}}
.
We assume additionally that
\eta
is an inaccessible cardinal of both
{\mathcal{Q}}
and
{\mathcal{R}}
and neither of
{\mathcal{Q}}
nor
{\mathcal{R}}
project across
\eta
.
The general case is not significantly different but needs some fine structure.

Let
{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Q}}
be the
id
-copy of
{\mathcal{U}}
onto
{\mathcal{Q}}
and
{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{R}}
be the
id
-copy of
{\mathcal{U}}
onto
{\mathcal{R}}
.
We have that
{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Q}}
and
{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{R}}
have the same tree order and use the same extenders
41
41

41

This is where we use the fact that
\eta
is an inaccessible cardinal in both
{\mathcal{Q}}
and
{\mathcal{R}}
and neither projects across
\eta
.
In general, we cannot conclude that
{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Q}}
and
{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{R}}
have the same extenders. However, we can simply apply
{\mathcal{U}}
to both
{\mathcal{Q}}
and
{\mathcal{R}}
, producing iterations trees
{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathcal{Q}}
and
{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathcal{R}}
such that
{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathcal{Q}}
and
{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathcal{R}}
have the same tree structure as
{\mathcal{U}}
and use exactly the same extenders.
We will then have that
{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathcal{Q}}
and
{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathcal{R}}
are pseudo-hulls of
{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathcal{Q}}
and
{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{R}}
, and so the proof can still be implemented. Here
{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathcal{Q}}
will have the following property: for every
\zeta<{\rm lh}({\mathcal{U}})
,
{\mathcal{M}}_{\zeta}^{{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathcal{Q}}}\trianglelefteq{\mathcal{M}}_{\zeta}^{{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Q}}}
.
The proof of this fact is very much like the proofs of
[
40
, Claim 6.1.7 and Claim 6.1.8]
.
.
Now let
{\mathcal{X}}_{0}
be the embedding normalization of
{\mathcal{T}}_{\leq\beta}\oplus{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Q}}
and
{\mathcal{X}}_{1}
be the embedding normalization of
{\mathcal{T}}\oplus{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{R}}
.
Since
{\mathcal{U}}
has limit length and
{\mathcal{Q}}|\eta={\mathcal{R}}|\eta
,
{\mathcal{X}}_{0}={\mathcal{X}}_{1}=_{def}{\mathcal{X}}
. Furthermore,
\Sigma_{{\mathcal{Q}}}({\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Q}})
and
\Sigma_{{\mathcal{R}}}({\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{R}})
are completely determined by
\Sigma({\mathcal{X}})
via exactly the same procedure (see
[
40
, Section 6.6]
and in particular,
[
40
, Corollary 6.6.20]
). Therefore,
\Sigma_{{\mathcal{Q}}}({\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{Q}})=\Sigma_{{\mathcal{R}}}({\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{R}})
.
∎

8.1.4.
The extender algebra

If
M
is a transitive model and
\delta
is a Woodin cardinal of
M
, let
{\sf{EA}}^{M}_{\delta}
denote the extender algebra of
V_{\delta}^{M}
at
\delta
with countably many generators.
If
\xi<\delta
and
w=[\xi,\delta]
, let
{\sf{EA}}^{M}_{w}
be the extender algebra of
V_{\delta}^{M}
that uses only extenders with critical points above
\xi
.
If
M
has a distinguished extender sequence, then we assume that the extender algebra uses extenders from that sequence.

\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}}(\nu)
is defined and
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}}
is
\mu
-bounded. Then
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}}
can be split into left and right components
{\mathcal{X}}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}
such that

(1)

the main branch of
{\mathcal{X}}
does not drop and
{\rm gen}({\mathcal{X}})\subseteq\pi_{{\mathcal{Q}},{\mathcal{R}}}(\nu)
,

(2)

if
{\mathfrak{w}}
is the last model of
{\mathcal{X}}
, then
\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{w}}}(\nu)=\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}}(\nu)
,

(3)

{\mathcal{Y}}
is a normal iteration tree on
{\mathfrak{w}}
that is strictly above
\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{w}}}(\nu)
, and

(4)

{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}}={\mathcal{X}}^{\frown}{\mathcal{Y}}
.

Proof.

Suppose
\gamma
is the least
\xi
such that
{\mathcal{M}}_{\xi}^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}}}|\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}}(\nu)={\mathcal{R}}|\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}}(\nu)
.
Set
{\mathcal{X}}=({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}})_{\leq\gamma}
.
It follows that
\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}}(\nu)=\pi_{0,\gamma}^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}}}(\nu)
, and therefore
\gamma
is on the main branch of
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}}
. Moreover,
({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}})_{\geq\gamma}
is strictly above
\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}}(\nu)
.
45
45

45

This follows from the facts that (1) in
{\mathcal{Q}}
,
\nu
is either a Woodin cardinal or a limit of Woodin cardinals and (2)
{\mathcal{Q}}
has no Woodin cardinals in the interval
(\nu,\mu)
.
So if
E
is an extender used in
({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}})_{\geq\gamma}
, then
{\rm crit}(E)>\nu
, as otherwise
{\mathfrak{r}}
would have a Woodin cardinal in the interval
(\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}}(\nu),\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}}(\mu))
.
Here we use the fact that
{\rm gen}({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}})\subseteq\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}}(\mu)
.
Therefore,
{\mathcal{X}}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}=_{def}({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}})_{\geq\gamma}
are as desired.
∎

Notation 9.3
.

Suppose
{\mathfrak{q}}=({\mathcal{Q}},\Lambda)
is a hod pair and
\nu\in{\mathcal{Q}}
.
Let

(1)

{\mathcal{T}}^{\nu}_{{\mathfrak{q}}}=({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty})_{\leq\xi}
, where
\xi
is the least
\xi^{\prime}
such that
{\mathcal{M}}_{\xi^{\prime}}^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}}||\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}(\nu)={\mathcal{M}}_{\infty}({\mathfrak{q}})||\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}(\nu)
, and

(2)

{\mathfrak{q}}^{\nu}
be the last model of
{\mathcal{T}}^{\nu}_{{\mathfrak{q}}}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}^{\nu}={\mathcal{M}}^{{\mathfrak{q}}^{\nu}}
.

In clause 1 of Notation
9.3
,
{\mathcal{T}}^{\nu}_{{\mathfrak{q}}}
is the longest initial segment
{\mathcal{U}}
of
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}
such that
{\rm gen}({\mathcal{U}})\subseteq\nu
. In most applications of Lemma
9.4
, we will have that
\nu\not\in{\rm dom}(\vec{E}^{\mathcal{Q}})
, and so in Notation
9.3
we could change the equality
{\mathcal{M}}_{\xi^{\prime}}^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}}||\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}(\nu)={\mathcal{M}}_{\infty}({\mathfrak{q}})||\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}(\nu)
to
{\mathcal{M}}_{\xi^{\prime}}^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}}|\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}(\nu)={\mathcal{M}}_{\infty}({\mathfrak{q}})|\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}(\nu)
46
46

46

Also, in the context of Lemma
9.4
, the equality
{\mathcal{M}}_{\xi^{\prime}}^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}}|\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}(\nu)={\mathcal{M}}_{\infty}({\mathfrak{q}})|\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}(\nu)
suffices. This is because if the lemma fails then it can be shown that
{\rm cf}(\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}(\nu))=\omega
which is clearly false.
.

Lemma 9.4
.

Suppose
({\mathfrak{q}},\nu,\xi)
are as in Notation
9.3
. Then
\xi
is on the main branch of
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}
.

Proof.

Suppose
\xi
is not on the main branch of
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}
. It follows that for some
\beta
,

(1.1)
{\rm crit}(E_{\beta}^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}})<\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}(\nu)
,

(1.2)
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}(\beta+1)<\xi
,

(1.3)
\beta+1
is on the main branch of
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}
, and

(1.4)
{\rm lh}(E_{\beta}^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}})\geq\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}(\nu)
.

Then if
\beta^{\prime}={\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}(\beta+1)
and
\beta^{\prime\prime}+1={\rm lh}({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty})
, then

\pi_{\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime\prime}}^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}}(\pi_{0,\beta^{\prime}}^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}}(\nu))>\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}(\nu)
, which is a contradiction.
∎

Remark 9.5
.

It will be convenient to also write
{\mathfrak{q}}^{\nu}
for
\nu\in{\mathcal{M}}_{\infty}({\mathfrak{q}})
such that
{\mathfrak{q}}
catches
\nu
. In this case, we set
{\mathfrak{q}}^{\nu}={\mathfrak{q}}^{\nu_{\mathfrak{q}}}
.

\mathcal{Q}^{\nu}_{\infty}
\mathcal{R}^{\nu_{\mathcal{R}}}_{\infty}
\mathcal{Q}
\mathcal{R}
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{Q},\mathcal{R}}
\mathcal{T}^{\nu}_{\mathcal{Q},\infty}
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{R},\infty}^{\nu_{\mathcal{R}}}
\mathcal{X}
\pi^{\mathcal{X}}

Figure 9.2.
Lemma
9.6
.

\mathcal{X}
is the full normalization of
({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}})^{\frown}{\mathcal{T}}^{\nu_{\mathfrak{r}}}_{{\mathfrak{r}},\infty}
, and
{\rm crit}(\pi^{\mathcal{X}}_{\xi,\zeta})>\nu_{\infty}
.

Lemma 9.6
.

Suppose
{\mathfrak{q}}=({\mathcal{Q}},\Lambda)
is a hod pair and
\nu
is a
{\mathcal{Q}}
-cardinal. Suppose
{\mathfrak{r}}=({\mathcal{R}},\Psi)
is a complete
\nu
-bounded iterate of
{\mathfrak{q}}
. Then
{\mathfrak{q}}^{\nu}={\mathfrak{r}}^{\nu_{\mathfrak{r}}}
.

Proof.

The proof follows from the fact that
{\rm gen}({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}})\subseteq\nu_{\mathfrak{r}}
, which implies that

{\mathcal{R}}=\{\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}}(f)(a):a\in[\nu_{\mathfrak{r}}]^{<\omega}\wedge f\in{\mathcal{Q}}\}.

Notice that
\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}(\nu)=\pi_{{\mathfrak{r}},\infty}(\nu_{\mathfrak{r}})
and, setting
\nu_{\infty}=\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}(\nu)
,
{\rm gen}({\mathcal{T}}^{\nu}_{{\mathfrak{q}}})\subseteq\nu_{\infty}
and
{\rm gen}({\mathcal{T}}^{\nu_{\mathfrak{r}}}_{{\mathfrak{r}}})\subseteq\nu_{\infty}
.

Let
{\mathcal{X}}
be the full normalization of
({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}})^{\frown}{\mathcal{T}}^{\nu_{\mathfrak{r}}}_{{\mathfrak{r}}}
.
Then if
\xi
is such that
{\mathcal{T}}^{\nu}_{{\mathfrak{q}}}=({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}}})_{\leq\xi+1}
, then
{\mathcal{T}}^{\nu}_{{\mathfrak{q}}}={\mathcal{X}}_{\leq\xi+1}
.

We claim that
\xi
is on the main branch of
{\mathcal{X}}
.
For since
\pi^{\mathcal{X}}(\nu)=\nu_{\infty}
, it follows from the argument of Lemma
9.4
that
\xi
is on the main branch of
{\mathcal{X}}
.
We then have that if
\zeta+1={\rm lh}({\mathcal{X}})
, then
{\rm crit}(\pi^{\mathcal{X}}_{\xi,\zeta})>\nu_{\infty}
.

We now claim that
\pi^{\mathcal{X}}_{\xi,\zeta}
is onto.
Let
y\in{\mathfrak{r}}^{\nu_{\mathfrak{r}}}_{\infty}
. Because
{\rm gen}({\mathcal{T}}^{\nu_{\mathfrak{r}}}_{{\mathfrak{r}}})\subseteq\nu_{\infty}
, we have that
y=\pi^{\nu_{\mathfrak{r}}}_{{\mathfrak{r}}}(g)(a)
, where
a\in[\nu_{\infty}]^{<\omega}
and
g\in{\mathcal{R}}
.
Because
{\rm gen}({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}})\subseteq\nu_{\mathfrak{r}}
, we have some
h\in{\mathcal{Q}}
and
b\in[\nu_{\infty}]^{<\omega}
such that
y=\pi^{\nu_{\mathfrak{r}}}_{{\mathfrak{r}}}(\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}}(h))(b)
.
Hence if
x=\pi^{\nu}_{{\mathfrak{q}}}(h)(b)
, then
\pi^{\mathcal{X}}_{\xi,\zeta}(x)=y
.
∎

(which implies that
