Corollary 9.7
.

Suppose that
{\mathfrak{q}}=({\mathcal{Q}},\Lambda)
is a hod pair and that
\mu
is a cardinal of
{\mathcal{Q}}
such that, letting

\nu=\sup(\{\nu^{\prime}<\mu:{\mathcal{Q}}\vDash``\nu^{\prime}
is a Woodin cardinal
"\})
,

\nu<\mu
. Suppose
{\mathfrak{r}}=({\mathcal{R}},\Psi)
is a complete
\mu
-bounded iterate of
{\mathfrak{q}}
. Then
{\mathfrak{r}}^{\nu_{\mathfrak{r}}}
is a normal iterate of
{\mathfrak{q}}^{\nu}
via a normal tree
{\mathcal{U}}
which is strictly above
\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}}}(\nu)
.

More precisely, setting
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}}={\mathcal{X}}^{\frown}{\mathcal{Y}}
, where
({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{Y}})
is as in Lemma
9.2
, and letting
F
be the long extender derived from
\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}\restriction{\mathcal{Q}}|(\nu^{+})^{\mathcal{Q}}
,
{\mathcal{U}}
is the minimal
({\mathcal{Q}},F)
-copy of
{\mathcal{U}}
.
47
47

47

More precisely,
F
consists of pairs
(a,A)
such that
a\in[\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}(\nu)]^{<\omega}
,
A\in{\mathcal{Q}}
and
a\in\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}(A)
.

9.2.
Small cardinals

The splitting of iterations trees into left and right components as described in Lemma
9.2
can be slightly generalized at a cost.
In Lemma
9.2
we did not make any smallness assumption on
{\mathcal{Q}}
.
Here we will introduce a splitting assuming
\mu
is
small
.
Small cardinals will appear throughout our analysis of Nairian models derived from hod mice.

The key ideas used in this subsection are now standard in the inner model theoretic literature.

Terminology 9.8
.

Suppose
{\mathfrak{q}}=({\mathcal{Q}},\Lambda)
is a hod pair and
\delta
is a cutpoint Woodin cardinal of
{\mathcal{Q}}
.

(1)

We say
\delta

ends an lsa block
of
{\mathcal{Q}}
if the least
{<}\delta
-strong cardinal of
{\mathcal{Q}}
is a limit of Woodin cardinals of
{\mathcal{Q}}
.
48
48

48

This terminology is justified by
[
25
]
.

(2)

Given
\nu<\delta
, we say
\nu
is in the
\delta
-block
of
{\mathcal{Q}}
if, letting
\kappa
be the least
{<}\delta
-strong cardinal of
{\mathcal{Q}}
,
\nu\in[\kappa,\delta]
.

(3)

Assuming
\delta
ends an lsa block of
{\mathcal{Q}}
, given
\nu
in the
\delta
-block of
{\mathcal{Q}}
, we say
\nu
is
big
in
{\mathcal{Q}}
(relative to
\delta
49
49

49

Usually
\delta
will be clear from the context.
) if there is a
\tau\leq\nu
such that
{\mathcal{Q}}\vDash``\tau
is a measurable cardinal that is a limit of
{<}\delta
-strong cardinals”.

(4)

For
\nu<\delta
as above, we say
\nu
is
small
in
{\mathcal{Q}}
if it is not big in
{\mathcal{Q}}
.

(5)

Given a
{\mathcal{Q}}
-cardinal
\nu
, we say
\nu
is
properly overlapped
in
{\mathcal{Q}}
if there is a
\delta^{\prime}
such that
\delta^{\prime}
is a Woodin cardinal of
{\mathcal{Q}}
,
\delta^{\prime}
ends an lsa block of
{\mathcal{Q}}
,
\nu
is in the
\delta^{\prime}
-block of
{\mathcal{Q}}
, and for all
\kappa\leq\nu
,
{\mathcal{Q}}|\nu\vDash``\kappa
is a strong cardinal” if and only if
{\mathcal{Q}}\vDash``\kappa
is a
{<}\delta
-strong cardinal.”
50
50

50

Our convention is that the large cardinal notions are witnessed by the extenders on the sequence of the relevant fine structural models.

(6)

Given a
{\mathcal{Q}}
-cardinal
\nu
, we say
\nu
is a
proper cutpoint
in
{\mathcal{Q}}
if
\nu
is properly overlapped and is not a critical point of a total extender of
{\mathcal{Q}}
.

Recall Terminology
8.6
.
Lemma
9.9
isolates the main property of small ordinals that we will use.

Lemma 9.9
.

Suppose
{\mathfrak{q}}=({\mathcal{Q}},\Lambda)
is a hod pair and
\delta
ends an lsa block of
{\mathcal{Q}}
. Suppose

(a)

\mu
is in the
\delta
-block of
{\mathcal{Q}}
and is a properly overlapped small regular cardinal of
{\mathcal{Q}}
,

(b)

{\mathfrak{r}}=({\mathcal{R}},\Psi)
is a complete iterate of
{\mathfrak{q}}
,

(c)

\nu\leq\mu_{\mathfrak{r}}
is a
{<}\delta_{\mathfrak{r}}
-strong cardinal of
{\mathcal{R}}
or a limit of such cardinals,

(d)

\zeta
is the least such that
{\mathcal{M}}_{\zeta}^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}}}|\nu={\mathcal{R}}|\nu
, and

(e)

\zeta^{\prime}
is the least such that
{\mathcal{M}}_{\zeta^{\prime}}^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}}}|\mu_{\mathfrak{r}}={\mathcal{R}}|\mu_{\mathfrak{r}}
.

Set
{\mathcal{S}}={\mathcal{M}}_{\zeta}^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}}}
,
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}={\mathcal{M}}_{\zeta^{\prime}}^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}}}
,
{\mathfrak{s}}=({\mathcal{S}},\Lambda_{\mathcal{S}})
and
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}=({\mathcal{S}}^{\prime},\Lambda_{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}})
. Then

(1)

\mu_{{\mathfrak{r}}}=\mu_{{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}}
,

(2)

({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}})_{\geq\zeta}
is a normal iteration tree on
{\mathcal{S}}
according to
\Lambda_{\mathcal{S}}
,

(3)

({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}})_{\geq\zeta^{\prime}}
is a normal iteration tree on
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
according to
\Lambda_{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}}
,

(4)

both
\zeta
and
\zeta^{\prime}
are on the main branch of
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}}
,

(5)

{\rm gen}({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{s}}})\subseteq\nu
,

(6)

{\rm gen}({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}})\subseteq\mu_{{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}}
,

(7)

({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}})_{\geq\zeta}
is strictly above
\nu
, and

(8)

({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}})_{\geq\zeta^{\prime}}
is strictly above
\mu_{{\mathfrak{r}}}
.

Proof.

We assume that
\nu<\mu_{{\mathfrak{r}}}
; the other case is easier.
Some of the clauses above have either been already verified or are immediate consequences of the others.
Clauses 1, 2 and 3 follow from clauses 7 and 8.
Part of clause 4, namely that
\zeta^{\prime}
is on the main branch of
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}}
, is essentially Lemma
9.4
.
However, clause 4 follows from clauses 7 and 8.
Clauses 5 and 6 follow from the definitions of
\zeta
and
\zeta^{\prime}
.
That leaves clauses 7 and 8.
Since their proofs are very similar, we demonstrate clause 7, which is the harder of the two.

Suppose that there is an extender
E
used in
({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}})_{\geq\zeta}
with
{\rm crit}(E)\leq\nu
.
Let
\alpha\in[\zeta,{\rm lh}({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}}))
be such that
E=E_{\alpha}^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}}}
. To save ink, we drop
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}}
from superscripts.
We have that

(1.1)
{\rm crit}(E_{\alpha})\leq\nu<{\rm im}(E_{\alpha})
, and

(1.2)
{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}|{\rm im}(E_{\alpha})={\mathcal{R}}|{\rm im}(E_{\alpha})
.

It follows from clause (c) that
51
51

51

{\rm im}(E_{\alpha})
is an inaccessible cardinal in
{\mathcal{R}}
and
\nu<{\rm im}(E_{\alpha})
is a strong cardinal or a limit of strong cardinals of
{\mathcal{R}}
. See also (1.2).

(2) there is
\eta\in[{\rm crit}(E_{\alpha}),\nu]
such that
{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}|{\rm im}(E_{\alpha})\vDash``\eta
is a strong cardinal.”

(2) and (1.1)-(1.2) imply that

(3.1)
{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}|{\rm crit}(E_{\alpha})\vDash``
there is a class of strong cardinals,” and

(3.2) if
{\rm crit}(E_{\alpha})<\nu
, then
{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}|\nu\vDash``{\rm crit}(E_{\alpha})
is a strong cardinal.”

Since
{\rm crit}(E_{\alpha})
is a measurable cardinal of
{\mathcal{M}}_{\alpha}
and
{\rm crit}(E_{\alpha})\leq\nu
, clause (c) implies that
{\mathcal{R}}\vDash``\mu_{\mathfrak{r}}
is big.”
∎

Corollary
9.10
follows straightforwardly from Lemma
9.9
.
The terminology it introduces is inspired by the hull and definability properties used in the theory of the core model (see
[
35
]
).

Corollary 9.10
.

Suppose
{\mathfrak{q}}=({\mathcal{Q}},\Lambda)
is a hod pair,
{\mathfrak{r}}=({\mathcal{R}},\Lambda_{\mathcal{R}})
is a complete iterate of
{\mathfrak{q}}
,
\delta
ends an lsa block of
{\mathcal{Q}}
, and
\mu
is such that either

(1)

\mu\in{\rm rge}(\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}}\restriction\delta)
, or

(2)

\mu
is in the
\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}}(\delta)
-block of
{\mathcal{R}}
,
\mu
is small in
{\mathcal{R}}
, and
\mu
is either a
<\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}}(\delta)
-strong cardinal of
{\mathcal{R}}
or a limit of such cardinals.

Then the following properties hold.

(1)

Hull Property:

\mathscr{P}(\mu)\cap{\mathcal{R}}\subseteq Hull^{{\mathcal{R}}|\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}}(\delta)}({\rm rge}(\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}}|\delta_{\mathfrak{q}},\infty})\cup\mu)
.

(2)

Definability Property:

\mu\in Hull^{{\mathcal{R}}}({\rm rge}(\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}}|\delta_{\mathfrak{q}},\infty})\cup\mu)
.

The hull and definability properties imply the next corollary via standard arguments.

Corollary 9.11
.

Suppose

(1)

{\mathfrak{p}}=({\mathcal{P}},\Sigma)
is a hod pair,

(2)

\delta
is a Woodin cardinal of
{\mathcal{P}}
that ends an lsa block,

(3)

{\mathfrak{q}}=({\mathcal{Q}},\Sigma_{\mathcal{Q}})
and
{\mathfrak{r}}=({\mathcal{R}},\Sigma_{\mathcal{R}})
are two complete iterates of
{\mathfrak{p}}
,

(4)

\nu
is in the
\delta
-block of
{\mathcal{P}}
,
\nu
is small in
{\mathcal{P}}
, and
\nu
is either a
<\delta
-strong cardinal of
{\mathcal{P}}
or a limit of such cardinals, and

(5)

\nu_{\mathfrak{q}}=\nu_{\mathfrak{r}}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}|\nu_{\mathfrak{q}}={\mathcal{R}}|\nu_{\mathfrak{r}}
.

Then the least-extender-disagreement-coiteration of
({\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}})
is strictly above
\nu_{\mathfrak{q}}
.

Proof.

Let
{\mathfrak{s}}=({\mathcal{S}},\Sigma_{\mathcal{S}})
be the common complete iterate of
{\mathfrak{q}}
and
{\mathfrak{r}}
obtained via the least-extender-disagreement-coiteration of
({\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}})
.
We first show that
{\rm crit}(\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{s}}})>\nu_{\mathfrak{q}}
and
{\rm crit}(\pi_{{\mathfrak{r}},{\mathfrak{s}}})>\nu_{\mathfrak{r}}
.
Notice that if
{\rm crit}(\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{s}}})\leq\nu_{\mathfrak{q}}
, then
{\rm crit}(\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{s}}})
is a
<\delta_{\mathfrak{q}}
-strong cardinal of
{\mathcal{Q}}
.
Similarly, if
{\rm crit}(\pi_{{\mathfrak{r}},{\mathfrak{s}}})\leq\nu_{\mathfrak{r}}
, then
{\rm crit}(\pi_{{\mathfrak{r}},{\mathfrak{s}}})
is a
<\delta_{\mathfrak{r}}
-strong cardinal of
{\mathcal{R}}
.

Assume now that
{\rm crit}(\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{s}}})\leq\nu_{{\mathfrak{q}}}
.
The definability property implies that
{\rm crit}(\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{s}}})={\rm crit}(\pi_{{\mathfrak{r}},{\mathfrak{s}}})
, and moreover, that if
\zeta={\rm crit}(\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{s}}})
, then
\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{s}}}(\zeta)=\pi_{{\mathfrak{r}},{\mathfrak{s}}}(\zeta)
.
The hull property then implies that
\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{s}}}\restriction({\mathcal{Q}}|(\zeta^{+})^{\mathcal{Q}})=\pi_{{\mathfrak{r}},{\mathfrak{s}}}\restriction({\mathcal{R}}|(\zeta^{+})^{\mathcal{R}})
.
Therefore, the first extender used on the main branch of
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{s}}}
is compatible with the first extender used on the main branch of
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{r}},{\mathfrak{s}}}
.

We now have that
{\rm crit}(\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{s}}})>\nu_{\mathfrak{q}}
and
{\rm crit}(\pi_{{\mathfrak{r}},{\mathfrak{s}}})>\nu_{\mathfrak{r}}
.
It follows that
\nu_{\mathfrak{q}}
is a
<\delta_{{\mathfrak{s}}}
-strong cardinal of
{\mathcal{S}}
. Because
\nu
is small in
{\mathcal{P}}
, it follows from Lemma
9.9
that
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{s}}}
is strictly above
\nu_{\mathfrak{q}}
.
A similar argument shows that
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{r}},{\mathfrak{s}}}
is strictly above
\nu_{\mathfrak{r}}
.
∎

Lemma
9.12
is another consequence of the hull and definability properties.
It essentially says that in least-extender-disagreement-coiterations, small strong cardinals can only be used on one side of the coiteration.

Lemma 9.12
.

Suppose

(1)

{\mathfrak{p}}=({\mathcal{P}},\Sigma)
is a hod pair,

(2)

\delta
is a Woodin cardinal of
{\mathcal{P}}
that ends an lsa block,

(3)

{\mathfrak{q}}=({\mathcal{Q}},\Sigma_{\mathcal{Q}})
and
{\mathfrak{r}}=({\mathcal{R}},\Sigma_{\mathcal{R}})
are two complete iterates of
{\mathfrak{p}}
,

(4)

{\mathfrak{s}}=({\mathcal{S}},\Sigma_{\mathcal{S}})
is the common iterate of
{\mathfrak{q}}
and
{\mathfrak{r}}
obtained via the least-extender-disagreement-coiteration of
({\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}})
, and

(5)

\zeta
is a small
{<}\pi_{{\mathfrak{p}},{\mathfrak{s}}}(\delta)
-strong cardinal of
{\mathcal{S}}
such that if
\alpha<{\rm lh}({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{s}}})
is the least with the property that
\zeta\in{\rm rge}(\pi^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{s}}}}_{\alpha})
, then letting
\zeta_{{\mathfrak{q}}}
be the
\pi^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{s}}}}_{\alpha}
-preimage of
\zeta
,
\sup(\pi^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{s}}}}_{\alpha}[\zeta_{{\mathfrak{q}}}])<\zeta
.
52
52

52

I.e., somewhere along the main branch of
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{s}}}
an extender with critical point the image of
\zeta_{\mathfrak{q}}
is used.
More precisely, there is
\beta\in[\alpha,{\rm lh}({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{s}}}))
such that
\beta
is on the main branch of
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{s}}}
and
{\rm crit}(E_{\beta}^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{s}}}})=\pi_{\alpha,\beta}^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{s}}}}(\zeta_{\mathfrak{q}})
.

Let now
\beta

Nowhere along the main branch (and since

Proof.

Notice that there are

Claim 9.13
.

Let
H
be the extender used on the main branch of
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{s}}}
at
\nu
. Then

Proof.

Set
{\mathfrak{w}}={\mathfrak{m}}_{\nu}^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{s}}}}
,

{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{r}},{\mathfrak{s}}}

and
\zeta_{{\mathfrak{w}}^{\prime}}=\iota_{\alpha,\nu^{\prime}}(\zeta_{\mathfrak{q}})
.
Towards a contradiction, assume that the claim is false.
Then
j_{\beta,\iota_{\mathfrak{r}}}(\zeta_{\mathfrak{r}})=i_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}(f)(a)
, where

and

It follows from Lemma

(6.1)

Notice next that the following statements hold:

55

55

This is because

9.3.
Bounding preimages and closure points

Lemma
9.14
bounds pre-images of points in the direct limit.

Lemma 9.14
.

Suppose
{\mathfrak{q}}=({\mathcal{Q}},\Lambda)
is a hod pair,
\delta
ends an lsa block of
{\mathcal{Q}}
, and
\mu
is a small properly overlapped cardinal of
{\mathcal{Q}}
that is in the
\delta
-block of
{\mathcal{Q}}
.
Suppose
A\in\mathscr{P}(\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}(\mu))\cap{\mathcal{M}}_{\infty}({\mathfrak{q}})
and
\iota<\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}(\mu)
.
Then there is a complete iterate
{\mathfrak{r}}
of
{\mathfrak{q}}
such that
{\mathfrak{r}}
catches both
A
and
\iota
and
{\rm gen}({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}})\subseteq\mu_{\mathfrak{r}}
.
Moreover, if
\iota
is either a
{<}\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}(\mu)
-strong cardinal of
{\mathcal{M}}_{\infty}({\mathfrak{q}})
or a limit of such cardinals, then there is a complete iterate
{\mathfrak{r}}
of
{\mathfrak{q}}
catching
\iota
and such that
{\rm gen}({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}})\subseteq\iota_{{\mathfrak{r}}}
.

Proof.

Given any complete iterate
{\mathfrak{r}}^{\prime}
of
{\mathfrak{q}}
that catches
(A,\iota)
, letting
{\mathcal{U}}
be the longest initial segment of
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}^{\prime}}
such that
{\rm gen}({\mathcal{U}})\subseteq\mu_{{\mathfrak{r}}^{\prime}}
and letting
{\mathfrak{r}}
be the last model of
{\mathcal{U}}
, it follows from Lemma
9.9
that
{\mathfrak{r}}
is as desired.

Suppose now that
\iota
is either a
{<}\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}(\mu)
-strong cardinal of
{\mathcal{M}}_{\infty}({\mathfrak{q}})
or a limit of such cardinals.
Lemma
9.9
implies that if
{\mathfrak{r}}^{\prime}
is a complete iterate of
{\mathfrak{q}}
that catches
\iota
and
\zeta
is the least such that
{\mathcal{M}}_{\zeta}^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}^{\prime}}}|\iota_{{\mathfrak{r}}^{\prime}}={\mathcal{M}}^{{\mathfrak{r}}^{\prime}}|\iota_{{\mathfrak{r}}^{\prime}}
, then
({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}^{\prime}})_{\geq\zeta}
is strictly above
\iota_{{\mathfrak{r}}^{\prime}}
.
It follows that if
{\mathfrak{r}}
is the last model of
({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}^{\prime}})_{\leq\zeta}
, then
{\mathfrak{r}}
catches
\iota
and
\iota_{{\mathfrak{r}}}=\iota_{{\mathfrak{r}}^{\prime}}
(this is because
{\mathfrak{r}}^{\prime}
is a complete iterate of
{\mathfrak{r}}
and
{\rm crit}(\pi_{{\mathfrak{r}},{\mathfrak{r}}^{\prime}})>\iota_{{\mathfrak{r}}^{\prime}}
).
Since
{\rm gen}({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}})\subseteq\iota_{{\mathfrak{r}}}
,
{\mathfrak{r}}
is as desired.
∎

Lemma
9.16
characterizes closure points.

Terminology 9.15
.

Suppose
{\mathfrak{p}}=({\mathcal{P}},\Sigma)
is a hod pair,
\delta
is a Woodin cardinal of
{\mathcal{P}}
that ends an lsa block of
{\mathcal{P}}
, and
\nu
is a
{<}\delta
-strong cardinal of
{\mathcal{P}}
. Let
\tau=(\nu^{+})^{\mathcal{P}}
,
{\mathfrak{q}}=({\mathcal{Q}},\Sigma_{\mathcal{Q}})
be a complete iterate of
{\mathfrak{p}}
, and
X_{\mathfrak{q}}=\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}[{\mathcal{Q}}|\tau_{\mathfrak{q}}]
.
We say
\gamma<\nu^{\infty}
is
({\mathfrak{q}},\nu)
-
closed
, or
(X_{\mathfrak{q}},\nu)
-
closed
, if

\gamma=\sup(\nu^{\infty}\cap Hull^{{\mathcal{M}}_{\infty}({\mathfrak{p}})|\tau^{\infty}}(X_{\mathfrak{q}}\cup\gamma))
.

We will often drop
\nu
from the notation and say that
\gamma
is
X_{\mathfrak{q}}
-closed to mean that
\gamma
is
{\mathfrak{q}}
-closed.
57
57

57

The idea here is that being
{\mathfrak{q}}
-closed is a property that can be defined using
(X_{\mathfrak{q}},\nu,\gamma)
, and that
{\mathfrak{q}}
is not needed.

Suppose
({\mathfrak{p}},\nu,\delta,\tau)
are as in Terminology
9.15
.
Notice that
\nu^{\infty}=\pi_{{\mathfrak{p}},\infty}(\nu)
is a limit of
({\mathfrak{q}},\nu)
-closed points and that if
\gamma
is
({\mathfrak{q}},\nu)
-closed, then if
{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}
is the transitive collapse of
Hull^{{\mathcal{M}}_{\infty}({\mathfrak{q}})|\tau^{\infty}}(X_{\mathfrak{q}}\cup\gamma)
and
\sigma:{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}\rightarrow{\mathcal{M}}_{\infty}({\mathfrak{q}})|\tau^{\infty}
is the uncollapse map then
{\rm crit}(\sigma)=\gamma
.
It follows that
{\mathcal{M}}_{\infty}({\mathfrak{q}})\vDash``\gamma
is properly overlapped.”
In fact, we have the following characterization of
{\mathfrak{q}}
-closed points.
Given a complete iterate
{\mathfrak{q}}
of
{\mathfrak{p}}
, we let
w_{\mathfrak{q}}=(\tau_{\mathfrak{q}},\delta_{\mathfrak{q}})
(this notation depends on
\tau
, but we will only use it below, and so we omit
\tau
).

Lemma 9.16
.

Suppose
({\mathfrak{p}},\nu,\delta,\tau)
are as in Terminology
9.15
. Suppose
{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}
is a complete iterate of
{\mathfrak{p}}
. Then
\gamma
is
({\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime},\nu)
-closed if and only if there is a complete iterate
{\mathfrak{r}}=({\mathcal{R}},\Sigma_{\mathcal{R}})
of
{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}
and an iterate
{\mathfrak{s}}=({\mathcal{S}},\Sigma_{\mathcal{S}})
of
{\mathfrak{r}}
such that

(1)

{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{r}},{\mathfrak{s}}}
is based on
w_{\mathfrak{r}}=(\tau_{\mathfrak{r}},\delta_{\mathfrak{r}})
,

(2)

{\rm gen}({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{p}},{\mathfrak{r}}})\leq\nu_{\mathfrak{r}}
, and

(3)

there is an extender
E\in\vec{E}^{{\mathcal{S}}}
such that
{\rm crit}(E)=\nu_{\mathfrak{r}}
,
{\rm gen}({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{r}},{\mathfrak{s}}})\leq{\rm im}(E)
, and
\pi_{{\mathfrak{r}}_{E},\infty}(\nu_{\mathfrak{r}})=\gamma
, where
{\mathfrak{r}}_{E}=(Ult({\mathcal{R}},E),\Sigma_{Ult({\mathcal{R}},E)})
.

In particular,
\nu^{\infty}
is a limit of
({\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime},\nu)
-closed points.

Proof.

Set
X=X_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}
.
58
58

58

This notation depends on
\nu
but we omit it as it is fixed.

We first prove the right-to-left direction.
Suppose that
\gamma=\pi_{{\mathfrak{r}}_{E},\infty}(\nu_{\mathfrak{r}})
for some
({\mathfrak{r}},{\mathfrak{s}},E)
as above.
We claim that
\gamma
is
X
-closed.
Towards a contradiction, assume that
\gamma<\sup(\nu^{\infty}\cap Hull^{{\mathcal{M}}_{\infty}({\mathfrak{p}})|\tau^{\infty}}(X\cup\gamma))
, and let

\gamma^{\prime}\in\nu^{\infty}\cap Hull^{{\mathcal{M}}_{\infty}({\mathfrak{p}})|\tau^{\infty}}(X\cup\gamma)

be such that
\gamma^{\prime}\geq\gamma
.
Let
s\in X^{<\omega}
,
t\in\gamma^{<\omega}
, and
\sigma
be a term such that
\gamma^{\prime}=\sigma^{{\mathcal{M}}_{\infty}({\mathfrak{p}})|\tau^{\infty}}(s,t)
.
Set
{\mathcal{R}}_{E}=Ult({\mathcal{R}},E)
, and let
{\mathfrak{q}}=({\mathcal{Q}},\Sigma_{\mathcal{Q}})
be a complete iterate of
{\mathfrak{r}}_{E}=({\mathcal{R}}_{E},\Sigma_{{\mathcal{R}}_{E}})
such that
t\in{\rm rge}(\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty})
(and hence,
\gamma^{\prime}\in{\rm rge}(\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty})
).
Setting

U=\{\sigma^{{\mathcal{R}}_{E}|\tau_{{\mathcal{R}}_{E}}}(s_{{\mathfrak{r}}_{E}},v):v\in(\nu_{\mathfrak{r}})^{<\omega}\}\cap\nu_{{\mathfrak{r}}_{E}}
,

we have
U\subseteq\nu_{\mathfrak{r}}
, as
U\subseteq{\rm rge}(\pi_{E}^{\mathcal{R}})
(because
\pi_{{\mathfrak{r}},{\mathfrak{r}}_{E}}(\nu_{\mathfrak{r}})=\nu_{{\mathfrak{r}}_{E}}
).
Notice also that
U\in{\mathcal{R}}_{E}
.
However, since
\gamma_{\mathfrak{q}}=\pi_{{\mathfrak{r}}_{E},{\mathfrak{q}}}(\nu_{\mathfrak{r}})
, we have that

{\mathcal{Q}}\vDash\exists\iota\in[\pi_{{\mathfrak{r}}_{E},{\mathfrak{q}}}(\nu_{\mathfrak{r}}),\nu_{{\mathfrak{q}}})\exists v\in(\pi_{{\mathfrak{r}}_{E},{\mathfrak{q}}}(\nu_{\mathfrak{r}}))^{<\omega}(\iota=\sigma^{{\mathcal{Q}}|\tau_{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(s_{{\mathfrak{q}}},v))
.

Thus,
{\mathcal{Q}}\vDash\pi_{{\mathfrak{r}}_{E},{\mathfrak{q}}}(U)\cap[\pi_{{\mathfrak{r}}_{E},{\mathfrak{q}}}(\nu_{\mathfrak{r}}),\nu_{\mathfrak{q}})\not=\emptyset
, contradiction.

Suppose next that
\gamma
is an
X
-closed point.
Let
{\mathfrak{q}}
be a complete iterate of
{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}
such that
\gamma\in{\rm rge}(\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty})
.
We must have that
\gamma_{\mathfrak{q}}<\nu_{\mathfrak{q}}
.
For
\beta<{\rm lh}({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{q}}})
, let
{\mathfrak{p}}_{\beta}={\mathfrak{m}}_{\beta}^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{q}}}}
.
We assume that for no
\beta+1<{\rm lh}({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{q}}})
such that
[0,\beta)_{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{q}}}}\cap D^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{q}}}}=\emptyset
,
\gamma\in{\rm rge}(\pi_{{\mathfrak{p}}_{\beta},\infty})
.
It follows that if
\beta+1={\rm lh}({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{p}},{\mathfrak{q}}})
, then
\beta=\beta^{\prime}+1
, and if
\zeta
is the
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{p}},{\mathfrak{q}}}
-predecessor of
\beta
, then
{\rm crit}(E_{\beta^{\prime}}^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{p}},{\mathfrak{q}}}})=\nu_{{\mathfrak{p}}_{\zeta}}
(we use smallness of
\nu
here, see Lemma
9.9
).
Let
{\mathcal{R}}={\mathcal{M}}_{\zeta}^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{p}},{\mathfrak{q}}}}
,
{\mathcal{S}}={\mathcal{M}}_{\beta^{\prime}}^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{p}},{\mathfrak{q}}}}
, and
E=E_{\beta^{\prime}}^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{p}},{\mathfrak{q}}}}
.
Let
{\mathfrak{r}}=({\mathcal{R}},\Sigma_{\mathcal{R}})
and
{\mathfrak{s}}=({\mathcal{S}},\Sigma_{\mathcal{S}})
.

There are two cases.
If
\gamma_{\mathfrak{q}}=\nu_{\mathfrak{r}}
, then there is nothing to prove, as
({\mathfrak{r}},{\mathfrak{s}},E)
are as desired.
Suppose then that
\gamma_{\mathfrak{q}}\not=\nu_{\mathfrak{r}}
.
As
\gamma\not\in{\rm rge}(\pi_{{\mathfrak{r}},\infty})
, we have
\gamma_{\mathfrak{q}}\in(\nu_{\mathfrak{r}},\nu_{\mathfrak{q}})
.
Because
\gamma
is
X
-closed, it must be that
\gamma_{\mathfrak{q}}<{\rm gen}(E)
, as otherwise
\gamma_{\mathfrak{q}}=\pi_{{\mathfrak{r}},{\mathfrak{q}}}(f)(s)
, where
s\in\gamma_{\mathfrak{q}}^{<\omega}
and
f\in\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}^{-1}[X]
.
It follows that
\gamma_{\mathfrak{q}}
must be a generator of
E
and hence,

{\mathcal{M}}_{\infty}({\mathfrak{p}})\vDash``\gamma
is overlapped by an extender with critical point
\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}(\nu_{\mathfrak{r}})"
.

Because
\gamma
is properly overlapped in
{\mathcal{M}}_{\infty}({\mathfrak{p}})
, we have that
{\mathcal{M}}_{\infty}({\mathfrak{p}})\vDash``\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}(\nu_{\mathfrak{r}})
is
{<}\delta^{\infty}
-strong cardinal.”
This contradicts the smallness of
\nu_{\mathfrak{r}}
in
{\mathcal{R}}
, as we have that
{\mathcal{R}}\vDash``\nu_{\mathfrak{r}}
is a limit of
{<}\delta_{\mathfrak{r}}
-strong cardinals.”
∎

Essentially the same proof establishes Lemma
9.17
.

Lemma 9.17
.

Suppose
({\mathfrak{p}},\nu,\delta,\tau)
are as in Terminology
9.15
.
Suppose that
{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}
is a complete iterate of
{\mathfrak{p}}
and
X=X_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}}
. Suppose
\gamma
is a
({\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime},\nu)
-closed point and
{\mathfrak{q}}=({\mathcal{Q}},\Sigma_{\mathcal{Q}})
is a complete iterate of
{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}
such that
\sup(\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}[\nu_{\mathfrak{q}}])\leq\gamma
.
Let
{\mathfrak{s}}=({\mathcal{S}},\Sigma_{\mathcal{S}})
be a complete iterate of
{\mathfrak{q}}
such that
\gamma\in{\rm rge}(\pi_{{\mathfrak{s}},\infty})
.
Let
{\mathcal{R}}
be the least node on the main branch of
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{s}}}
such that
{\mathcal{R}}|\gamma_{\mathfrak{s}}={\mathcal{S}}|\gamma_{\mathfrak{s}}
, and let
\iota<{\rm lh}({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{s}}})
be such that
{\mathcal{R}}={\mathcal{M}}_{\iota}^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{s}}}}
. Let
{\mathfrak{r}}={\mathfrak{m}}_{\iota}^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{s}}}}
.

Then
\gamma_{\mathfrak{s}}=\nu_{\mathfrak{r}}
, and there is
\beta<{\rm lh}({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{s}}})
such that
\beta+1
is on the main branch of
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{s}}}
,
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{s}}}(\beta+1)=\iota
, and
{\rm crit}(E_{\beta}^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{s}}}})=\nu_{\mathfrak{r}}
.

10.
Nairian models derived from hod mice

In this section, we

(1)

prove a useful result about the Vopěnka algebra (Lemma
10.4
) and use it to establish a result showing that the different Nairian models agree on the powerset operation (see Theorem
10.6
, whose main proof ideas we use several times),

(2)

construct a Nairian model of
{\sf{ZF}}+``\omega_{1}
is a supercompact cardinal” (see Theorem
10.13
and Corollary
10.17
),

(3)

show that strong cardinals of
{\rm{HOD}}
are successor and regular cardinals of the Nairian model (see Theorem
10.20
and Theorem
10.23
), and

(4)

show that the cofinality of strong cardinals of
{\rm{HOD}}
is big in the parent determinacy model (Theorem
10.26
).

Additional notation for §
10

We use the following notation in addition to Notation

and Terminology

throughout this section.
We will be working inside the derived model of some hod mouse.
We assume as our working hypothesis that
{\mathcal{V}}\vDash{\sf{ZFC}}
is a hod premouse

We tacitly assume that all large cardinal notions are witnessed by the extenders on the extender sequence of hod premouse.
and

,

Let

10.2.
Bounding the Solovay sequence

We study the Solovay sequences of various Nairian models under the working hypothesis stated in Notation

.

Definition 10.1
.

Suppose
X_{0},X_{1}
are any two sets. We then let
\theta_{0}(X_{0},X_{1})
be the supremum of ordinals
\beta
such that there is an
OD(X_{1})

60

60

This means that
X_{1}
can be used as a parameter.
surjection
f:X_{0}\rightarrow\beta
.

While we will apply Definition

inside determinacy models, the definition also makes sense in
\mathsf{ZFC}
.

Lemma 10.2
.

Suppose
\nu<\lambda
,
{\mathfrak{q}}=({\mathcal{Q}},\Lambda)
is a complete iterate of
{\mathfrak{p}}
such that
\nu\in{\rm rge}(\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty})

.

Then

M\vDash``\mu\text{ is not a surjective image of an $OD(Z)$ function with domain }\mathscr{P}_{\omega_{1}}(\nu)."

Thus,

M\vDash\theta_{0}(\mathscr{P}_{\omega_{1}}(\nu),Z)\leq\mu.

Proof.

Assume not and suppose
f:\mathscr{P}_{\omega_{1}}(\nu)\rightarrow\mu
is an
OD(Z)^{M}
surjection.
We furthermore assume that
f
is the least such
OD(Z)^{M}
function. Let
h\subseteq Coll(\omega,{\mathcal{Q}}|\nu_{\mathfrak{q}})
be
{\mathcal{Q}}
-generic.

Let

such that there is
\beta<\mu_{\mathfrak{q}}
with the property that
(p_{0},p_{1})
forces the following statement:

(
*_{\beta}
) If
k*(x_{0},x_{1})
is the generic, then in
{\mathcal{Q}}[k*(x_{0},x_{1})]
,
(x_{0},x_{1})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
and, letting for
m\in 2
,
Y_{m}=\{\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}(k(i)):i\in x_{m}\},
61
61

61

Our intended reader knows the arguments why sets like

are definable over
{\mathcal{Q}}
.
Such arguments are part of the basic hod analysis.
In particular, the material explained in Section

implies that
{\mathcal{Q}}

knows

\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}\restriction({\mathcal{Q}}|\nu_{\mathfrak{q}})
, which is the portion of
\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}
that is needed to define
Y_{0}
and
Y_{1}
.
This is because we can find a complete iterate
{\mathfrak{q}}^{\prime}
of
{\mathfrak{q}}
such that

is above
\delta_{\mathfrak{q}}
, and
M
can be realized as the derived model of
{\mathcal{M}}^{{\mathfrak{q}}^{\prime}}
at
\eta_{{\mathfrak{q}}^{\prime}}
. But then, since

\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}\restriction({\mathcal{Q}}|\mu_{\mathfrak{q}})\in{\rm rge}(\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{q}}^{\prime}})
.

(a)

there is an
OD(Y_{0})^{M}
surjection
h:\mathscr{P}_{\omega_{1}}(\nu)\rightarrow\mu
, and

(b)

surjection then
f_{Y_{0}}(Y_{1})=\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}(\beta)
.

For
p\in\mathcal{A}
, let

be the unique
\beta
as above.
We have that
\left|\mathcal{A}\right|^{\mathcal{Q}}<\mu_{\mathfrak{q}}
, since the extender algebra is
\mu_{\mathfrak{q}}
-cc.

It is not hard to show that
\{\beta_{p}:p\in\mathcal{A}\}=\mu_{\mathfrak{q}}
. Indeed, fix
\beta<\mu_{\mathfrak{q}}
. Let
{\mathfrak{r}}=({\mathcal{R}},\Psi)
be a complete iterate of
{\mathfrak{q}}
such that for some
{\mathcal{R}}
-generic

and some

, setting for

,

, and

.
It readily follows that for some

Hence, for some

.
Contradiction.
∎

The following corollary also follows from the results of
[
38
]
. In particular see
[

, Theorem 5.3]
.

Corollary 10.3
.

Suppose
\nu<\xi^{\infty}
and
\mu
is the least Woodin cardinal of
{\mathcal{H}}
that is greater than
\nu
. Then for any
X\in\mathscr{P}_{\omega_{1}}(\nu)
,
\theta_{0}(\mathscr{P}_{\omega_{1}}(\nu),X)\leq\mu
.

Proof.

Fix an
X\in\mathscr{P}_{\omega_{1}}(\nu)
.
We want to see that
\theta_{0}(\mathscr{P}_{\omega_{1}}(\nu),X)\leq\mu
. We can fix a complete iterate
{\mathfrak{q}}=({\mathcal{Q}},\Lambda)
of
{\mathfrak{p}}
such that
X\subseteq\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}[{\mathcal{Q}}|\nu_{\mathfrak{q}}]
.
Let
Z=\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}[{\mathcal{Q}}|\nu_{\mathfrak{q}}]
, and fix a pair of reals
(u_{0},u_{1})
such that
u_{0}
codes a function
f:\omega\rightarrow{\mathcal{Q}}|\nu_{\mathfrak{q}}
and
X=\{\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}(f(i)):i\in u_{1}\}
.
Since
X
is ordinal definable from
(Z,u_{0},u_{1})
,
62
62

62

Notice that
Z\prec{\mathcal{H}}|\nu
, and so
Z
determines
\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}\restriction({\mathcal{Q}}|\nu_{\mathfrak{q}})
, as this is the inverse of the transitive collapse of
Z
.
it is enough to prove that
\theta_{0}(\mathscr{P}_{\omega_{1}}(\nu),(Z,u_{0},u_{1}))\leq\mu
, or just that for any real
u
,
\theta_{0}(\mathscr{P}_{\omega_{1}}(\nu),(Z,u))\leq\mu
.

To see this, suppose not and let
p
be a finite set of ordinals and
\phi
be a formula such that, letting
g:\mathscr{P}_{\omega_{1}}(\nu)\rightarrow\mu
be given by

g(Y)=\beta\mathrel{\leftrightarrow}\phi[Z,u,p,Y,\beta]
,

{\rm rge}(g)=\mu
.
Define
h:\mathscr{P}_{\omega_{1}}(\nu)\rightarrow\mu
by setting
h(Y)=\beta
if and only if, letting
Y\cap\omega=r
,
\phi[Z,r,p,Y,\beta]
holds.
Then
h
is ordinal definable from
Z
and
{\rm rge}(h)=\mu
, contradicting Lemma
10.2
.
∎

Lemma 10.4

(Vopenka Algebra)
.

Suppose
\nu<\xi^{\infty}
and
X\in\nu^{\omega}
.
Let
\mu
be the least Woodin cardinal of
{\mathcal{H}}
such that
\nu<\mu
. Then
X
is generic over
{\mathcal{H}}
for a poset of size
\mu
.

Proof.

Consider the Vopenka algebra for adding a member of
\mathscr{P}_{\omega_{1}}(\nu)
to
{\mathcal{H}}
.
Lemma
10.2
implies that this algebra has the
\mu
-cc in
{\mathcal{H}}
.
Indeed, if
\mathcal{A}\in{\mathcal{H}}
is an antichain in the aforementioned Vopenka algebra such that
\left|\mathcal{A}\right|^{\mathcal{H}}=\mu
, then we can find an
OD
sequence
(A_{i}:i<\mu)
such that
A_{i}\subseteq\mathscr{P}_{\omega_{1}}(\nu)
and, for
i\not=i^{\prime}
,
A_{i}\cap A_{i^{\prime}}=\emptyset
.
Define
h:\mathscr{P}_{\omega_{1}}(\nu)\rightarrow\mu
by letting
h(Y)
be the least
i
such that
Y\in A_{i}
, if there is such an
i
, and otherwise set
h(Y)=0
.
Then
h
is an
OD
-surjection, contradicting Lemma
10.2
.

Because
\mu
is an inaccessible cardinal in
{\mathcal{H}}
, we can find
\mathbb{P}\in{\mathcal{H}}
of size at most
\mu
with
X\in{\mathcal{H}}[G]
for some
G\subseteq\mathbb{P}
.
Indeed, work in
\mathcal{H}
.
Let
\mathbb{Q}
be the Vopenka algebra.
Let
f:\omega\rightarrow X
be an enumeration of
X
, and let
\sigma
be a
\mathbb{Q}
-name for
f
such that for some sequence of maximal
\mathbb{Q}
-antichains
(A_{n}:n<\omega)
,
\sigma
consists of tuples
(p,\check{n},\check{\alpha})
such that
p\in A_{n}
.
Let
\mathbb{P}_{0}=\cup_{n<\omega}A_{n}
.
We can then inductively construct
(\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}:\alpha\leq\mu)
such that

(1)

for each
\alpha<\mu
,
\left|\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}\right|<\mu
and
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}
is a sub-poset of
\mathbb{Q}
,

(2)

for
\alpha<\beta
,
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}\subseteq\mathbb{P}_{\beta}
,

(3)

for
\zeta\leq\mu
a limit ordinal,
\mathbb{P}_{\zeta}=\bigcup_{\alpha<\zeta}\mathbb{P}_{\alpha}
, and

(4)

if
A\subseteq\mathbb{P}_{\mu}
is a maximal antichain, then
A
is also a maximal antichain in
\mathbb{Q}
.

Then
\mathbb{P}_{\mu}
is the desired
\mathbb{P}
.
In fact, if
G\subseteq\mathbb{Q}
is
{\mathcal{H}}
-generic and is such that
\sigma_{G}=f
, then
G^{\prime}=G\cap\mathbb{P}
is also
{\mathcal{H}}
-generic and
\sigma_{G^{\prime}}=f
.
∎

10.3.
The agreement of the powersets

The following lemma is due to Woodin, although it is easier to prove in our context.

Lemma 10.5
.

Suppose
\beta<\xi^{\infty}
and
X\in\mathscr{P}_{\omega_{1}}(\beta)
.
Then
{\mathcal{H}}[X]=({\sf{HOD}}_{X})^{M}
.
In particular,
V_{\xi^{\infty}}^{{\mathcal{H}}[X]}=V_{\xi^{\infty}}^{({\sf{HOD}}_{X})^{M}}
.

Proof.

Recall that
M
can be embedded into the derived model of
{\rm{HOD}}
at
\Theta
,
63
63

63

This is a general theorem of Woodin, but in our context it follows from
{\rm{HOD}}
analysis. However, see
[
2
]
.
and moreover, there is such an embedding
j^{*}
such that
j=_{def}j^{*}\restriction{\rm{HOD}}\in{\rm{HOD}}
.
64
64

64

j
is really just the
{\mathcal{H}}
-to-
j^{*}({\mathcal{H}})
iteration embedding.
j^{*}
is then given by mapping the homogenity systems via
j
.
More precisely, if
B\subseteq{\mathbb{R}}
is a set in
M
and
\bar{\mu}
is a homogeneity system such that
B=S_{\bar{\mu}}^{M}
, then for
s\in\omega^{<\omega}
, letting
k_{s}=\pi_{\bar{\mu}_{s}}\restriction{\mathcal{H}}
,
j^{*}(B)
is the set of
y
such that the direct limit of
(j(k_{y\restriction m}):m\in\omega)
is well-founded.

Clearly
{\mathcal{H}}[X]\subseteq({\sf{HOD}}_{X})^{M}
.
Let
A
be an
OD[X]
-definable set of ordinals.
Notice that
j^{*}(X)=j^{*}[X]=j[X]
, and so
j[X]\in{\mathcal{H}}[X]
. Therefore,
j^{*}(A)\in{\mathcal{H}}[X]
.

A
is definable in
{\mathcal{H}}[X]
via the equivalence
\beta\in A\mathrel{\leftrightarrow}j(\beta)\in j^{*}(A)
.
∎

Theorem
10.6
is the first major step towards the proof of Theorem
8.1
.
It shows that different Nairian models compute the powerset operation correctly.
(Below, we use
{\mathcal{H}}
for
{\rm{HOD}}
.)

Theorem 10.6
.

For every
\beta<\xi^{\infty}
,

\mathscr{P}(\beta^{\omega})\cap{\mathrm{N}}^{+}=\mathscr{P}(\beta^{\omega})\cap{\mathrm{N}}=\mathscr{P}(\beta^{\omega})\cap{\mathrm{N}}^{-}.

Proof.

Fix
A\subseteq\mathscr{P}(\beta^{\omega})\cap{\mathrm{N}}^{+}
. Let
\gamma<\xi^{\infty}
be such that
A
is
OD(X)
for some
X\in\mathscr{P}_{\omega_{1}}(\gamma)
. We can assume without losing generality that
\gamma\geq\beta
. Let
(j,j^{*})
be as in the proof of Lemma
10.5
.

Now
{\rm{HOD}}_{X}
is an infinity-Borel code for
A
, i.e., there is a formula
\phi
and a finite sequence of ordinals
s
such that

Y\in A\mathrel{\leftrightarrow}{\rm{HOD}}_{X}[Y]\vDash\phi[s,X,Y,j\restriction({\mathcal{H}}|\Theta)].

To see this, let
s\in{\sf{Ord}}^{<\omega}
be such that
A
is definable from
(X,s)
, and let
\psi(u_{0},u_{1},u_{2})
be the formula defining
A
from
(X,s)
over
M
. Thus,
Y\in A\mathrel{\leftrightarrow}M\vDash\psi[s,X,Y]
. We then have that for any
Y\in\mathscr{P}_{\omega_{1}}(\beta)
,

Y\in A\mathrel{\leftrightarrow}{\rm{HOD}}_{X}[Y]\vDash``\psi[j(s),j[X],j[Y]]
holds in the derived model at
\Theta
.”

The equivalence holds because, since
X
and
Y
are in
\mathscr{P}_{\omega_{1}}(\gamma)
, we have
j^{*}(X)=j[X]
and
j^{*}(Y)=j[Y]
.
We can now find a formula
\phi
such that

Y\in A\mathrel{\leftrightarrow}{\mathcal{H}}[X][Y]\vDash\phi[s,X,Y,j\restriction({\mathcal{H}}|\Theta^{M})].

Let
\mu
be the least Woodin cardinal of
{\mathcal{H}}
that is above
\gamma
.
It follows from Lemma
10.4
that if
Y\in\mathscr{P}_{\omega_{1}}(\beta)
, then
Y
can be added to
{\mathcal{H}}[X]
by a poset of size at most
\mu
. Working in
M
, let
\zeta=((\Theta^{+}))^{{\rm{HOD}}}
and
i:{\mathcal{M}}\rightarrow H_{\zeta}^{{\rm{HOD}}}
be such that

(1)

(i,{\mathcal{M}})\in{{\rm{HOD}}}
and
\{s,j\restriction({\mathcal{H}}|\Theta^{M})\}\in{\rm rge}(i)
,

(2)

{\rm crit}(i)>(\mu^{+})^{{\rm{HOD}}}
, and

(3)

\left|{\mathcal{M}}\right|^{\rm{HOD}}=(\mu^{+})^{\rm{HOD}}
.

Then every
Y\in\mathscr{P}_{\omega_{1}}(\beta)
can be added to
{\mathcal{M}}
by a poset of size at most
\mu
, and for each such
Y
,
i
extends to
i_{Y}:{\mathcal{M}}[Y]\rightarrow H_{\zeta}^{{\rm{HOD}}}[Y]
. Let
k=i^{-1}(j\restriction({\mathcal{H}}|\Theta^{M})
and
t=i^{-1}(s)
.
It now follows that

Y\in A\mathrel{\leftrightarrow}{\mathcal{M}}[X][Y]\vDash\phi[t,X,Y,k].

Therefore,
A\in{\mathrm{N}}^{-}
.
∎

Corollary 10.7
.

For
\alpha<\beta<\xi^{\infty}
, let
\Omega^{\alpha,\beta}=\Omega_{{\mathcal{H}}|\alpha}\restriction{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\beta}
. Then the function
(\alpha,\beta)\mapsto\Omega^{\alpha,\beta}
is definable over
({\mathrm{N}}^{-},{\mathcal{H}}|\xi^{\infty},\in)
.

Proof.

This is an application of generic interpretability (see
[
40
]
and
[
23
]
). Fix
\alpha<\beta<\xi
.
Define
\Omega^{\alpha,\beta}
uniformly in
(\alpha,\beta)
over
({\mathrm{N}}^{-},{\mathcal{H}}|\xi^{\infty},\in)
.
Let
{\mathcal{K}}={\mathcal{H}}|\xi^{\infty}
.
Let
{\mathcal{T}}\in{\mathrm{N}}^{-}
be a normal iteration tree according to
\Omega^{\alpha,\beta}
. We then set
\Omega^{\alpha,\beta}({\mathcal{T}})=b
if and only if
{\mathcal{K}}[{\mathcal{T}}]\vDash S^{{\mathcal{K}}}({\mathcal{T}})=b
.
65
65

65

S^{\mathcal{M}}
is the internal strategy predicate of
{\mathcal{M}}
. We use
S^{\mathcal{M}}
also for its interpretation onto generic extensions.

Our definition makes sense because
{\mathcal{T}}
is ordinal definable from some
X\in\mathscr{P}_{\omega_{1}}(\xi^{\infty})
, and so
{\mathcal{T}}\in{\mathcal{K}}[X]
implying that
{\mathcal{K}}[{\mathcal{T}}]
makes sense.
∎

Theorem
10.6
can be used to show e.g. that in the full Nairian model, the successor of the first
\omega
-many strongs is not a strong cardinal, see
[
7
]
.

10.4.
Regularity of
\xi^{\infty}

Theorem 10.8
.

Suppose
\xi
is an inaccessible cardinal in
{\mathcal{P}}
.
Then
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}\vDash{\rm cf}(\xi^{\infty})=\xi^{\infty}
.

Proof.

Suppose
f:\beta\rightarrow\xi^{\infty}
is an increasing cofinal function such that
f\in{\mathrm{N}}^{+}
.
We want to see that
\beta=\xi^{\infty}
.
Suppose that
\beta<\xi^{\infty}
.
Fix some
\beta^{\prime}<\xi^{\infty}
and
X\in\mathscr{P}_{\omega_{1}}({\mathcal{M}}_{\infty}|\beta^{\prime})
such that
f
is ordinal definable in
M
from
X
.
Without losing generality, we may assume that
M\vDash``f
is the
OD(X)
-least surjection.”

Fix
{\mathfrak{q}}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}
such that
{\mathfrak{q}}^{\prime}
catches
\beta,\beta^{\prime}
and
X\subseteq\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}}^{\prime},\infty}[{\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}|\beta^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{q}}^{\prime}}]
where
{\mathfrak{q}}^{\prime}=({\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime},\Lambda^{\prime})
.
Let
\zeta_{0}<\zeta^{\prime}_{1}<\xi_{{\mathfrak{q}}^{\prime}}
be two Woodin cardinals of
{\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}
such that
\beta^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{q}}^{\prime}}<\zeta_{0}
(and hence,
X\subseteq\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}}^{\prime},\infty}[{\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}|\zeta_{0}]
).
Setting
Z=\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}}^{\prime},\infty}\restriction({\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}|\zeta_{0})
, let
(w_{0},w_{1})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
be a
Z
-
code
of
X
.
More precisely,
w_{0}
codes a surjection
k:\omega\rightarrow{\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}|\zeta_{0}
and
X=\{Z(k(i)):i\in w_{1}\}
.
Let

66

66

This is the extender algebra at

Lemma 10.9
.

X\in{\mathcal{Q}}[w_{0},w_{1}]
and
{\mathcal{Q}}[w_{0},w_{1}]\vDash``\xi_{\mathfrak{q}}
is an inaccessible limit of Woodin cardinals”.

Proof.

The second claim is a consequence of the fact that
(w_{0},w_{1})
is added to
{\mathcal{Q}}
by a forcing whose
{\mathcal{Q}}
-cardinality is
<\xi_{\mathfrak{q}}
.
The first is an immediate consequence of Proposition

and the fact that

\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}}^{\prime},\infty}\restriction({\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}|\zeta_{0})=\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}\restriction({\mathcal{Q}}|\zeta_{0}).

∎

It follows from Proposition
8.12
that the derived model of
{\mathcal{Q}}
at
\eta_{\mathcal{Q}}
is
M
.
It then follows from Lemma

that
f\in{\mathcal{Q}}[w_{0},w_{1}]
.

Now work inside
{\mathcal{Q}}
.
Let
u=(\zeta_{0},\zeta_{1})
, and let

be the set of pairs
(p,\alpha)
such that

(1)

,

(2)

\alpha<\beta_{\mathfrak{q}}
, and

(3)

p

is such that

.

Define

as follows.
Given

, set
k(p,\alpha)=\gamma
just in case

forces that if
(x_{0},x_{1})
is the generic pair then, letting

(a)

increasing and cofinal function”,

\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}(\gamma)=f_{U}(\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}(\alpha))
.
If
p

.

We claim that

is cofinal. To see this, fix
\gamma<\xi_{\mathfrak{q}}
. We want to show that there is

such that

. Let
{\mathfrak{r}}=({\mathcal{R}},\Psi)
be a complete iterate of
{\mathfrak{q}}
such that for some

,
\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\infty}(\gamma)\leq f(\pi_{{\mathfrak{r}},\infty}(\alpha^{\prime}))
.

Lemma 10.10
.

{\mathcal{R}}\vDash``\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}}(\gamma)\leq\sup({\rm rge}(\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}}(k)))."

Proof.

Fix
(x_{0},x_{1})
such that
x_{0}
codes a surjection
n:\omega\rightarrow{\mathcal{R}}|\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}}(\zeta_{0})
and
X=\{\pi_{{\mathfrak{r}},\infty}(n(i)):i\in x_{1}\}
.
Let
{\mathfrak{s}}=({\mathcal{S}},\Phi)
be a complete iterate of
{\mathfrak{r}}
based on the window

such that the pair
(x_{0},x_{1})
is generic over
{\mathcal{S}}
for
\sf{EA}_{\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{s}}}(u)}^{{\mathcal{S}}}
.
We can find a complete iterate

of
{\mathfrak{s}}
that is obtained by iterating above

and is a genericity iterate of
{\mathcal{P}}
(see Definition

).
We then have that

(1)

and
\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{s}}}(k)=\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}}(k)
, and

(2)

{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}\vDash``\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}}(\gamma)\leq\sup({\rm rge}(\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}}(k)))"
.

Therefore,
{\mathcal{S}}\vDash``\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{s}}}(\gamma)\leq\sup({\rm rge}(\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{s}}}(k))),"
which implies

{\mathcal{R}}\vDash``\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}}(\gamma)\leq\sup({\rm rge}(\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},{\mathfrak{r}}}(k)))."

∎

But pulling back to
{\mathcal{Q}}
, we have that
{\mathcal{Q}}\vDash``\gamma\leq\sup({\rm rge}(k))"
.
Thus,
k
is cofinal.
But
{\mathcal{Q}}\vDash``\xi_{\mathfrak{q}}
is an inaccessible cardinal.” Contradiction.
∎

Corollary 10.11
.

Suppose
\xi
is an inaccessible cardinal in
{\mathcal{P}}
.
Then
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}\vDash\mathsf{ZF}-{\sf{Powerset}}
.

Proof.

It is enough to prove that
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}\vDash{\sf{Replacement}}
.
To show this, we will in fact show that if
B\in{\mathrm{N}}^{-}
and

is in
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}
, then
{\rm rge}(f)\in{\mathrm{N}}^{-}
.
This easily follows from the regularity of
\xi^{\infty}
.
Indeed, because
B\in{\mathrm{N}}^{-}
, for some
\beta<\xi^{\infty}
, we have a surjection
h:\mathscr{P}_{\omega_{1}}(\beta)\rightarrow B
with
h\in{\mathrm{N}}^{-}
.
67
67

67

First find
\beta<\xi^{\infty}
such that
B\in{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\beta}
.
Next let
(\alpha_{i}:i<\omega)
be the first
\omega
indiscernibles of
{\mathrm{N}}_{\beta}
.
Notice that whenever
y\in{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\beta}
, there is an
n<\omega
and
Z\in\beta^{\omega}
such that
y
is definable from
((\alpha_{i}:i\leq n),Z,{\mathcal{H}}|\beta)
in
{\mathrm{N}}_{\beta}
.
For
n,m\in\omega
, let

be such that letting
s_{n}=(\alpha_{0},...,\alpha_{n})
and
\phi_{m}
be the formula coded by
m
, for every
U\in{\rm dom}(h_{n,m})
,
h_{n,m}(U)=a
if and only if

is the unique

such that

.
We can now code the sequence
(h_{n,m}:(n,m)\in\omega^{2})
into one surjection

.

Let

be such that for each

,
k(Y)
is the least ordinal
\tau
such that

.
Fix some

and

such that in
M
,
h
is ordinal definable from
X
, and consider the prewellordering
\leq^{*}
of
\mathscr{P}_{\omega_{1}}(\beta)
given by

if and only if

.
We have that the length of

is less than

by Corollary

.
Because

is regular in

, it follows that for some

, we have

.

Now fix a surjection

with

, and consider the set

consisting of those

such that, letting

and

.
Then for some

Therefore,

∎

10.5.

.

{\mathrm{N}}^{D^{{\mathcal{Q}}^{+},\eta}}_{\epsilon}\vDash\psi[\dot{Z},\dot{W},\dot{w}].

.

Corollary 10.17
.

Suppose
\xi
is an inaccessible cardinal of
{\mathcal{P}}
that is also a limit of
<\delta
-strong cardinals of
{\mathcal{P}}
.
Then
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}\vDash``{\sf{ZF}}+``\omega_{1}
is a supercompact cardinal.”

Proof.

We first prove that
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}\vDash{\sf{ZF}}
.
By Corollary
10.11
, it suffices to verify that
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}
satisfies the Powerset Axiom.
Suppose
X\in{\mathrm{N}}^{-}
.
We need to see that there is
Y\in{\mathrm{N}}^{-}
such that
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}\vDash\mathscr{P}(X)=Y
.
First fix some
\beta<\xi^{\infty}
such that
X\in{\mathrm{N}}_{\beta}
.
It then follows that there is a surjection
F:\beta^{\omega}\rightarrow X
with
F\in{\mathrm{N}}^{-}
.
Theorem
10.13
implies that
\mathscr{P}(\beta^{\omega})\in{\mathrm{N}}^{-}
, and
hence
\mathscr{P}(X)=\{F[a]:a\in\mathscr{P}(\beta^{\omega})\}\in{\mathrm{N}}^{-}
.

To see that
{\mathrm{N}}^{-}\vDash``\omega_{1}
is a supercompact cardinal,” we let
\mu_{\alpha}\in M
be the
\omega_{1}
-supercompactness measure on
\mathscr{P}_{\omega_{1}}(\alpha^{\omega})
, for
\alpha<\xi^{\infty}
.
It is enough to show that for each
\alpha<\xi^{\infty}
,
\mu_{\alpha}\cap{\mathrm{N}}^{-}\in{\mathrm{N}}^{-}
.
Fix such an
\alpha<\xi^{\infty}
, and let
\lambda
be the third least
<\delta^{\infty}
-strong cardinal of
{\mathcal{H}}
that is greater than
\alpha
.
We have that
\mathscr{P}(\alpha^{\omega})\cap{\mathrm{N}}^{-}\in{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\lambda}
.
It follows from a result of Woodin that
\mu_{\alpha}
is ordinal definable (see
[
49
]
), and hence, fixing a surjection
G:\lambda^{\omega}\rightarrow\mathscr{P}(\alpha^{\omega})\cap{\mathrm{N}}^{-}_{\lambda}
with
G\in{\mathrm{N}}^{-}
, we have that the set
A=\{z\in\lambda^{\omega}:G(z)\in\mu_{\alpha}\}
is ordinal definable from
G
.
Hence
A\in{\mathrm{N}}^{-}
(see Theorem
10.6
).
∎

We need the following version of Theorem
10.13
which appears as
[
38
, Theorem 6.3(a)]
.

Theorem 10.18
.

If
\nu
is the first
{<}\delta^{\infty}
-strong cardinal of
{\mathcal{H}}
, then
\mathscr{P}(\omega^{\omega})\cap{\mathrm{N}}_{\nu}=\mathscr{P}(\omega^{\omega})\cap{\mathrm{N}}
.

The proof of Theorem
10.18
is very similar to, but much easier than, the proof of Theorem
10.13
.
We do not need to consider the Skolem hulls as we did in Lemma
10.16
.
Nor do we need to push
{\mathfrak{q}}
up to level
\tau
.
We only need to consider
{\mathfrak{q}}
itself and define
\gamma
,
\beta
,
\mu
, and
\zeta
relative to
{\mathfrak{q}}
via the same definition.
The set
A
that we need to consider is simply a set of reals, and so the term relation
\sigma
that we consider only needs to add a code for the parameter
W
, and the pair
(u_{0},u_{1})
can be substituted with just a single
u\in{\mathbb{R}}
.
Lemma
10.16
is easier now as we can simply show that
A
can be defined using
{\mathfrak{q}}|\zeta
and a pair of reals
(v_{0},v_{1})
coding the parameter
X
using the method illustrated in (7.3) and (7.4) of the proof of Theorem
10.13
.
We leave the details to the reader.

10.6.
Strong cardinals are regular cardinals

We next show that all small strong cardinals
<\xi^{\infty}
are regular cardinals in
{\mathrm{N}}
.

Lemma 10.19
.

Suppose
\nu
is in the
\delta
-block of
{\mathcal{P}}
and is a
<\delta
-strong cardinal of
{\mathcal{P}}
.
Suppose
\alpha\in((\nu^{+})^{\mathcal{P}},\delta)
and
{\mathfrak{q}}

{\mathfrak{p}}
such that
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{p}},{\mathfrak{q}}}

.
Let

{\rm lh}(E)\in(\alpha,\delta_{\mathfrak{q}})
and

.
Set
{\mathcal{P}}_{E}=Ult({\mathcal{P}},E)
,
{\mathcal{Q}}_{E}=Ult({\mathcal{Q}},E)
,

, and
{\mathfrak{q}}_{E}=({\mathcal{Q}}_{E},\Sigma_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{E}})
.
Then
\pi_{{\mathfrak{p}}_{E},\infty}\restriction(\nu^{+})^{\mathcal{P}}=\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}}_{E},\infty}\restriction(\nu^{+})^{\mathcal{P}}
.

Proof.

Let
{\mathcal{U}}=({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{p}},{\mathfrak{q}}})^{n\frown}E
78
78

78

See Notation
8.3
.
and
{\mathcal{X}}
be the full normalization of

.
∎

Theorem 10.20
.

Suppose

i=\pi^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{r}}_{E},{\mathfrak{s}}}}\circ\pi_{E}
and
j=\pi_{F}\circ\pi^{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{r}},{\mathfrak{w}}}}
. We have that
i=j
. Hence,
\sup(i[\nu_{\mathfrak{r}}])=\sup(j[\nu_{\mathfrak{r}}])
. But (6) implies that
\sup(i[\nu_{\mathfrak{r}}])=\pi_{{\mathfrak{r}}_{E},{\mathfrak{s}}}(\nu_{\mathfrak{r}})
and
\sup(j[\nu_{\mathfrak{r}}])=\pi_{{\mathfrak{r}},{\mathfrak{w}}}(\nu_{\mathfrak{r}})=\nu_{\mathfrak{w}}
. Therefore,
\pi_{{\mathfrak{r}}_{E},{\mathfrak{s}}}(\nu_{\mathfrak{r}})=\nu_{\mathfrak{w}}
.
and
\pi_{{\mathfrak{w}}_{F},\infty}(\nu_{\mathfrak{w}})=\pi_{{\mathfrak{s}},\infty}(\nu_{\mathfrak{w}})
.

It then follows that

(10)
\pi_{{\mathfrak{r}}_{E},\infty}(\nu_{\mathfrak{r}})=\pi_{{\mathfrak{s}},\infty}(\nu_{\mathfrak{w}})
.

Since
{\rm crit}(F)=\nu_{\mathfrak{w}}>\iota_{{\mathfrak{s}}}
, we have that

(11)
{\mathfrak{w}}
catches
\iota
and
\iota_{\mathfrak{w}}=\iota_{\mathfrak{s}}
.

The next claim finishes the proof.

Claim 10.22
.

f(\iota)<\pi_{{\mathfrak{r}}_{E},\infty}(\nu_{\mathfrak{r}})

Proof.

By (10), it is enough to show that

(12)
f(\iota)<\pi_{{\mathfrak{s}},\infty}(\nu_{\mathfrak{w}})
.

Let
u_{0}
be a real coding a surjection
g_{u_{0}}:\omega\rightarrow{\mathcal{W}}|\alpha_{\mathfrak{w}}
and
u_{1}\subseteq\omega
be such that
X=(\pi_{{\mathfrak{w}},\infty}(g_{u_{0}}(i)):i\in\omega)
.
We can find a complete iterate
{\mathfrak{w}}^{\prime}=({\mathcal{W}}^{\prime},\Sigma_{{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}})
of
{\mathfrak{w}}
such that
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{w}},{\mathfrak{w}}^{\prime}}
is based on the interval
(\alpha_{\mathfrak{w}},\tau_{\mathfrak{w}})
and for some generic
h\subseteq Coll(\omega,\tau_{{\mathfrak{w}}^{\prime}})
,
(u_{0},u_{1})\in{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}[h]
.
Then it follows from the definition of
\gamma^{{\mathfrak{w}}^{\prime}}
that

(13)
f(\iota)\leq\gamma^{{\mathfrak{w}}^{\prime}}
.

Using (8) we get that, letting
G=\pi_{{\mathfrak{w}},{\mathfrak{w}}^{\prime}}(F)
and
{\mathfrak{w}}^{\prime}_{G}=(Ult({\mathcal{W}}^{\prime},G),\Sigma_{Ult({\mathcal{W}}^{\prime},G)})
,

(14)
\gamma^{{\mathfrak{w}}^{\prime}}<\pi_{{\mathfrak{w}}^{\prime}_{G},\infty}(\nu_{{\mathfrak{w}}^{\prime}})
.

Then it follows from (10), (13) and (14) that it is enough to establish

(15)
\pi_{{\mathfrak{s}},\infty}(\nu_{\mathfrak{w}})=\pi_{{\mathfrak{w}}^{\prime}_{G},\infty}(\nu_{{\mathfrak{w}}^{\prime}})

Let
{\mathcal{U}}
be the result of applying
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{w}},{\mathfrak{w}}^{\prime}}
to
{\mathfrak{s}}
.
Thus
{\mathcal{U}}
has the same extenders and tree structure as
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{w}},{\mathfrak{w}}^{\prime}}
.
Let
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}
be the last model of
{\mathcal{U}}
.
Then the full normalization of
F\oplus{\mathcal{U}}
has the form
({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{w}},{\mathfrak{w}}^{\prime}})^{n\frown}G^{\frown}{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}
, where
{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}
is on
Ult({\mathcal{W}},G)
, is above
\pi_{G}(\nu_{\mathfrak{w}})
, and is the minimal
G
-copy of
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{w}},{\mathfrak{w}}^{\prime}}
.
Similarly, the full normalization of
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{w}},{\mathfrak{w}}^{\prime}}\oplus G
is
({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{w}},{\mathfrak{w}}^{\prime}})^{n\frown}G^{\frown}{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}
.
Thus the full normalization of
F\oplus{\mathcal{U}}
and of
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{w}},{\mathfrak{w}}^{\prime}}\oplus G
is
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{w}},{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}}
.
It follows that
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}={\mathfrak{w}}^{\prime}_{G}
and
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}}={\mathcal{U}}
is above
\nu_{\mathfrak{w}}
.
Thus

(16.1)
\pi_{{\mathfrak{s}},\infty}(\nu_{\mathfrak{w}})=\pi_{{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime},\infty}(\nu_{\mathfrak{w}})
,

(16.2)
\nu_{\mathfrak{w}}=\nu_{{\mathfrak{w}}^{\prime}}
.

Since
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}={\mathfrak{w}}^{\prime}_{G}
, (16.1) gives (15).
∎

This proves the lemma.
∎

Theorem
10.20
is proved.
∎

10.7.
Small strongs are successor cardinals

Theorem 10.23
.

Suppose
\nu<\xi^{\infty}
is in the
\delta^{\infty}
-block of
{\mathcal{H}}
and is a small strong cardinal of
{\mathcal{H}}
.
Let
\lambda
be the supremum of all

-strong cardinals of

that are strictly less than
\nu
.
If
\lambda
is a
{<}\delta^{\infty}
-strong cardinal of

{\mathrm{N}}\vDash\nu=\lambda^{+}

Proof.

Let

.
It is enough to prove that for some

Claim 10.25
.

\pi_{{\mathfrak{x}}_{0},{\mathfrak{r}}^{\lambda}_{0}}\restriction{\mathcal{M}}^{{\mathfrak{x}}_{0}}|\lambda_{{\mathfrak{x}}_{0}}=\pi_{{\mathfrak{x}}_{1},{\mathfrak{r}}^{\lambda}_{1}}\restriction{\mathcal{M}}^{{\mathfrak{x}}_{1}}|\lambda_{{\mathfrak{x}}_{1}}

10.8.
Small strongs have big cofinality

The main result of this section is that the cofinality of strong cardinals
<\xi^{\infty}
is
\Theta^{\mathrm{N}}
.

Theorem 10.26
.

Suppose
\nu<\delta^{\infty}
is a small
{<}\delta^{\infty}
-strong cardinal of
{\mathcal{H}}
, and let
\kappa
be the least
{<}\delta^{\infty}
-strong cardinal of
{\mathcal{H}}
.
Then
M\vDash``{\rm cf}(\nu)=\kappa."

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we assume that
{\mathfrak{p}}
catches
\nu
.
Let
\nu^{\prime}=(\nu^{+})^{\mathcal{H}}
and
w=(\nu^{\prime},\delta^{\infty})
. We say that
\alpha
is a
\kappa
-trace
of
\nu
if there is a complete iterate
{\mathfrak{q}}=({\mathcal{Q}},\Sigma_{\mathcal{Q}})
of
{\mathfrak{p}}
such that

(1.1)
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{p}},{\mathfrak{q}}}
is based on
w_{\mathfrak{p}}
, and

(1.2) for some
{\mathcal{Q}}
-inaccessible cardinal
\zeta\in w_{\mathfrak{q}}
,
\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}}|\zeta,\infty}(\kappa_{\mathfrak{p}})=\alpha
.

Define
f:\kappa\rightarrow\nu
by setting
f(\alpha)=\kappa
if
\alpha
is not a
\kappa
-trace of
\nu
, and if
\alpha
is a
\kappa
-trace of
\nu
, then, letting
{\mathfrak{q}}=({\mathcal{Q}},\Sigma_{\mathcal{Q}})
and
\zeta
witness that
\alpha
is a
\kappa
-trace, and letting
E\in\vec{E}
be the least such that
\zeta<{\rm lh}(E)
and
{\rm crit}(E)=\nu_{\mathfrak{q}}
, set
f(\alpha)=\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}}_{E},\infty}(\nu_{\mathfrak{q}})
92
92

92

Notice that
\nu_{{\mathfrak{q}}}=\nu_{\mathfrak{p}}
. It follows from Lemma
10.19
that
\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}}_{E},\infty}(\nu_{\mathfrak{q}})=\pi_{{\mathfrak{p}}_{E},\infty}(\nu_{\mathfrak{p}})
.
, where
{\mathcal{Q}}_{E}=Ult({\mathcal{Q}},E)
and
{\mathfrak{q}}_{E}=({\mathcal{Q}}_{E},\Sigma_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{E}})
.

Lemma
10.19
, Theorem
11.1
and Corollary

imply that
{\rm rge}(f)
is cofinal in
\nu
, so it is enough to show that

(a)

f
is a function, and

(b)

f
is increasing.

We first show (a).
To show that
f
is a function, it is enough to show that if
\alpha
is a
\kappa
-trace of
\nu
, then

is independent of the witness
({\mathfrak{q}},\zeta)
.
Let

-trace of

.
Let

and

, and let

and

.
Let
{\mathcal{Q}}_{E}=Ult({\mathcal{Q}},E)
,

,
{\mathcal{R}}_{F}=Ult({\mathcal{R}},F)
and
{\mathfrak{r}}_{F}=({\mathcal{R}}_{F},\Sigma_{{\mathcal{R}}_{F}})
.
We want to see that
\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}}_{E},\infty}(\nu_{\mathfrak{p}})=\pi_{{\mathfrak{r}}_{F},\infty}(\nu_{\mathfrak{p}})
.

We can assume that
{\rm gen}({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{p}},{\mathfrak{q}}})\subseteq\zeta
and
{\rm gen}({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{p}},{\mathfrak{r}}})\subseteq\tau
.
[Indeed, notice that it follows from Lemma
10.19
that if

\iota<{\rm lh}({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{p}},{\mathfrak{q}}})

and

. ]

Our strategy is to compare

Since

,

and

93

93

Notice that
\kappa_{\mathfrak{q}}=\kappa_{\mathfrak{r}}=\kappa_{\mathfrak{p}}
.

94

94

Indeed, we have that the full normalization of

(4.4)
{\mathfrak{p}}_{H}|\zeta={\mathfrak{q}}|\zeta
and
\pi_{{\mathfrak{p}}_{H}|\zeta,\infty}=\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}}|\zeta,\infty}
.

Let
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}
be the longest initial segment of
{\mathcal{T}}
such that
{\rm gen}({\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})\subseteq\nu^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{k}}}
and
{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}
be the longest initial segment of
{\mathcal{U}}
such that
{\rm gen}({\mathcal{U}}^{\prime})\subseteq\nu^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{k}}}
.

Let
({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{Y}})
be the iteration trees produced via the least-extender-disagreement-coiteration of
({\mathfrak{p}}_{H}|\zeta,{\mathfrak{r}}_{E}|\tau_{{\mathfrak{r}}_{E}})
. Because
\zeta
is inaccessible in
{\mathcal{P}}_{H}
and
\tau_{{\mathfrak{r}}_{E}}
is a regular cardinal in
{\mathcal{R}}_{E}
, we have that
{\mathcal{X}}^{{\mathcal{P}}_{H}}\trianglelefteq{\mathcal{T}}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}^{{\mathcal{R}}_{E}}\trianglelefteq{\mathcal{U}}
, where
{\mathcal{X}}^{{\mathcal{P}}_{H}}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}^{{\mathcal{R}}_{E}}
are the
id
-copies of
{\mathcal{X}}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}
onto
{\mathcal{P}}_{H}
and
{\mathcal{R}}_{E}
.
In what follows we will abuse our notation and let
{\mathcal{X}}^{{\mathcal{P}}_{H}}={\mathcal{X}}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}^{{\mathcal{R}}_{E}}={\mathcal{Y}}
.

Sublemma 11.3
.

{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}\trianglelefteq{\mathcal{X}}
and
{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}\trianglelefteq{\mathcal{Y}}
.

Proof.

Let
S
be the set of all
\lambda\leq\nu^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{k}}}
such that
\lambda
is a
{<}\delta_{\mathfrak{k}}
-strong cardinal of
{\mathcal{K}}
or a limit of such cardinals.
We prove by induction on
\lambda\in S
that if
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{\lambda}
and
{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}_{\lambda}
are the longest initial segments of
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}
and
{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}
with the property that
{\rm gen}({\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{\lambda})\subseteq\lambda
and
{\rm gen}({\mathcal{U}}^{\prime})\subseteq\lambda
, then
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{\lambda}\trianglelefteq{\mathcal{X}}
and
{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}_{\lambda}\trianglelefteq{\mathcal{Y}}
.
This follows from Corollary
9.11
and Lemma
9.12
.
We then define, for
\lambda\in S
, the statements

{\sf{IH}}_{\lambda}
: for all
\lambda^{\prime}\in S\cap\lambda
,
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{\lambda^{\prime}}\trianglelefteq{\mathcal{X}}
and
{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}_{\lambda^{\prime}}\trianglelefteq{\mathcal{Y}}
,

and

{\sf{IH}}(\lambda)
:
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{\lambda}\trianglelefteq{\mathcal{X}}
and
{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}_{\lambda}\trianglelefteq{\mathcal{Y}}
.

Since we are allowing padding, for any
\lambda\in S
and for any
\epsilon
,

\epsilon\leq{\rm lh}({\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{\lambda})
and
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{\lambda}\restriction\epsilon\triangleleft{\mathcal{X}}
if and only if
\epsilon\leq{\rm lh}({\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}_{\lambda})
and
{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}_{\lambda}\restriction\epsilon\triangleleft{\mathcal{Y}}
.

It follows from clause (3) of Lemma
9.9
that if
\lambda
is a limit point of
S
, then
{\sf{IH}}_{\lambda}
implies
{\sf{IH}}(\lambda)
.

11.1.1.
Verifying
{\sf{IH}}(\lambda)
for
\lambda=\min(S)
.

Assume first that
\lambda
is the least
{<}\delta_{\mathfrak{k}}
-strong cardinal.
In what follows, we will use
\lambda_{\mathfrak{m}}
to denote the image or the pre-image of
\lambda
in various iterates
{\mathfrak{m}}
of
{\mathfrak{p}}
.
Letting
{\mathcal{X}}_{0}
be the longest initial segment of
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{p}},{\mathfrak{r}}_{E}}
such that
{\rm gen}({\mathcal{X}}_{0})\subseteq\sup(\pi_{{\mathfrak{p}},{\mathfrak{r}}_{E}}[\lambda_{\mathfrak{p}}])
,
{\mathcal{X}}_{0}\trianglelefteq{\mathcal{X}}
,
97
97

97

Here we abuse the notation and assume that
{\mathcal{X}}_{0}
is an iteration tree on
{\mathcal{P}}_{H}
. The abuse is warranted because
{\mathcal{P}}|\lambda_{\mathfrak{p}}={\mathcal{P}}_{H}|\lambda_{{\mathfrak{p}}_{H}}
. We will do this sort of abuse of notation whenever it helps to keep the notation simple.
and if
\iota_{0}={\rm lh}({\mathcal{X}}_{0})
, then
{\mathcal{Y}}_{<\iota_{0}}
has no extenders and is obtained by padding.
There are two cases.

Case 1.

\sup(\pi_{{\mathfrak{p}},{\mathfrak{r}}_{E}}[\lambda_{\mathfrak{p}}])=\lambda_{{\mathfrak{r}}_{E}}
.
In this case, it follows from Corollary
9.11
that
\lambda_{{\mathfrak{r}}_{E}}=\lambda
and both
{\mathcal{X}}_{\geq\iota_{0}}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}_{\geq\iota_{0}}
are strictly above
\lambda
.
It follows that
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{\lambda}={\mathcal{X}}_{0}
and
{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}_{\lambda}={\mathcal{Y}}_{<\iota_{0}}
, and the claim is proved.

Case 2.

\sup(\pi_{{\mathfrak{p}},{\mathfrak{r}}_{E}}[\lambda_{\mathfrak{p}}])<\lambda_{{\mathfrak{r}}_{E}}
.
In this case, since
\lambda_{{\mathfrak{p}}_{H}}=\lambda_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
and
{\mathfrak{p}}_{H}|\lambda_{{\mathfrak{p}}_{H}}={\mathfrak{p}}|\lambda_{\mathfrak{p}}
, it follows that
\sup(\pi_{{\mathfrak{p}}_{H},{\mathfrak{k}}}[\lambda_{{\mathfrak{p}}_{H}}])<\lambda
.
Lemma
9.12
then implies that

\sup(\pi_{{\mathfrak{r}}_{E},{\mathfrak{k}}}[\lambda_{{\mathfrak{r}}_{E}}])=\lambda.

Therefore,
{\mathcal{Y}}
never uses an extender whose critical point is a pre-image of
\lambda
.
This means that if
{\mathcal{Y}}_{0}
is the longest initial segment of
{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}
such that
{\rm gen}({\mathcal{Y}}_{0})\subseteq\lambda
,
{\mathcal{Y}}_{0}
is based on
{\mathcal{R}}_{E}|\lambda_{{\mathfrak{r}}_{E}}
.
Since
{\mathfrak{p}}_{H}|\zeta
must win the coiteration with
{\mathfrak{r}}_{E}|\tau_{{\mathfrak{r}}_{E}}
(see (3)), we have that
{\mathcal{Y}}_{0}\trianglelefteq{\mathcal{Y}}
, and thus if
\iota_{1}={\rm lh}({\mathcal{Y}}_{0})
, then
{\mathcal{T}}_{\leq\iota_{1}}
is based on
{\mathfrak{p}}_{H}|\zeta
and
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{\lambda}={\mathcal{X}}_{\leq\iota_{1}}
.
Once again, the claim follows.

98

98

Notice that

\rho^{\prime}=\sup(\pi_{{\mathfrak{r}}_{E},{\mathfrak{k}}}[\nu_{{\mathfrak{r}}_{E}}])

Claim 11.4
.

{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}={\mathcal{Y}}
.

Proof.

Let
{\mathfrak{s}}=({\mathcal{S}},\Sigma_{\mathcal{S}})
be the last model of
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}
and
{\mathfrak{w}}=({\mathcal{W}},\Sigma_{\mathcal{W}})
be the last model of
{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}
.
In light of Sublemma
11.3
,
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}
can be considered to be both an iteration tree on
{\mathfrak{p}}_{H}
and on
{\mathfrak{p}}_{H}|\zeta
.
To distinguish them, we let
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{0}
be the version of
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}
on
{\mathfrak{p}}_{H}
and
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{1}
be the version of
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}
on
{\mathfrak{p}}_{H}|\zeta
.

{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}
,
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{0}
and
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{1}
use the same extenders and have the same tree structure.
We treat
{\mathfrak{s}}
as the last model of
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{0}
.
Because
\zeta
is chosen to be inaccessible in
{\mathcal{Q}}
, it is inaccessible in
{\mathcal{P}}_{H}
, and so, letting
\mu=\pi_{{\mathfrak{p}}_{H},{\mathfrak{s}}}(\zeta)
,
{\mathfrak{s}}|\mu
is the last model of
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{1}
.
Similarly, if we let
{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}_{0}
be the version of
{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}
on
{\mathfrak{r}}_{E}
,
{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}_{1}
be the version of
{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}
on
{\mathfrak{r}}_{E}|\tau_{{\mathfrak{r}}_{E}}
, and treat
{\mathfrak{w}}
as the last model of
{\mathcal{U}}_{0}^{\prime}
, then the last model of
{\mathcal{U}}_{1}^{\prime}
is simply
{\mathfrak{w}}|\epsilon
, where
\epsilon=\pi_{{\mathfrak{r}}_{E},{\mathfrak{w}}}(\tau_{{\mathfrak{r}}_{E}})
.
Notice that (see Terminology
9.8
)

(5.1)
{\mathcal{S}}
does not have a
{<}\mu
-strong cardinal in the interval
(\nu^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{s}},\mu)
, and so
\mu
is properly overlapped in
{\mathcal{S}}
,

(5.2)
{\mathcal{W}}
does not have a
{<}\epsilon
-strong cardinal in the interval
(\nu^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{w}},\epsilon)
, and so
\epsilon
is properly overlapped in
{\mathcal{W}}
, and

(5.3)
{\mathcal{T}}_{\geq\iota^{\prime}}
and
{\mathcal{U}}_{\geq\iota^{\prime}}
are strictly above
\nu^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{s}}}=\nu^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{w}}}=\nu^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{k}}}
.

Then
{\mathcal{X}}_{\geq\iota^{\prime}}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}_{\leq\iota^{\prime}}
are the trees produced via the least-extender-disagreement-coiteration of
({\mathfrak{s}}|\mu,{\mathfrak{w}}|\epsilon)
.
Let
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}=({\mathcal{S}}^{\prime},\Sigma_{{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}})
and
{\mathfrak{w}}^{\prime}=({\mathcal{W}}^{\prime},\Sigma_{{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}})
be the last models of
{\mathcal{X}}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}
.
It follows from (3) that
{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}\triangleleft{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
and from (5.2) that
{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}|\rho^{\prime}={\mathcal{K}}|\rho^{\prime}
.
Hence
{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}={\mathcal{Y}}
.
∎

Let now
\zeta_{\mathfrak{k}}=\pi_{{\mathfrak{p}}_{H},{\mathfrak{k}}}(\zeta)
.
It follows from Sublemma
11.3
that
{\mathfrak{k}}|\zeta_{\mathfrak{k}}
is a complete iterate of
{\mathfrak{p}}_{H}|\zeta
, and it follows from Claim
11.4
that if
\rho^{\prime\prime}
is the successor of
\rho^{\prime}
in
{\mathcal{K}}
, then
{\mathfrak{k}}|\rho^{\prime}
is a complete iterate of
{\mathfrak{r}}_{E}|\tau_{{\mathfrak{r}}_{E}}
.
It follows from (3) that
\rho^{\prime}<\zeta_{{\mathfrak{k}}}
.
Notice that

{\mathcal{K}}\vDash``\text{$\pi^{\mathcal{T}}(\nu_{\mathfrak{p}})$ is ${<}\zeta_{{\mathfrak{k}}}$-strong and $\nu^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{k}}}<\pi^{\mathcal{T}}(\nu_{\mathfrak{p}})$,"}

while we also have that (see (5.2))

{\mathcal{K}}\vDash``\text{ there is no $<\pi^{{\mathcal{U}}}(\nu_{{\mathfrak{r}}})$-strong cardinal that is strictly greater than $\nu^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{k}}}$".}

It then follows that
\pi^{\mathcal{U}}(\nu_{{\mathfrak{r}}})<\pi^{\mathcal{T}}(\nu_{\mathfrak{p}})
(because
\pi^{\mathcal{U}}(\nu_{\mathfrak{r}})<\rho^{\prime}<\zeta_{\mathfrak{k}}
). This finishes the proof of Lemma
11.2
.
∎

We isolate exactly what the proof that Clause 1 implies Clause 2 established (the proof of Lemma

is essentially the proof of Corollary

).

Corollary 11.5
.

Let
\kappa
be the least
{<}\delta
-strong cardinal of
{\mathcal{P}}
,
\nu>\kappa
be a small
{<}\delta
-strong cardinal of
{\mathcal{P}}
, and
\alpha\in(\nu,\delta)
.
Set
w=(\alpha,\delta)
,
\tau=(\nu^{+})^{\mathcal{P}}
, and
X=\pi_{{\mathfrak{p}},\infty}[{\mathcal{P}}|\tau]
.
Suppose
\beta<\nu^{\infty}
is an
X
-closed point as witnessed by
({\mathfrak{r}},{\mathfrak{s}},E)
(see Lemma
9.16
).
Set
{\mathcal{R}}_{E}=Ult({\mathcal{R}},E)
and
{\mathfrak{r}}_{E}=({\mathcal{R}}_{E},\Sigma_{{\mathcal{R}}_{E}})
.
Suppose
{\mathfrak{q}}=({\mathcal{Q}},\Sigma_{\mathcal{Q}})
is a complete iterate of
{\mathfrak{p}}
such that
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{p}},{\mathfrak{q}}}
is based on
w_{\mathfrak{p}}
and for some
{\mathcal{Q}}
-inaccessible
\zeta\in w_{\mathcal{Q}}=_{def}(\alpha,\delta_{\mathcal{Q}})
,
\pi_{{\mathfrak{r}}_{E}|\tau_{{\mathfrak{r}}_{E}},\infty}(\nu_{\mathfrak{r}})<\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}}|\zeta,\infty}(\kappa)
.
Let
H\in\vec{E}^{\mathcal{Q}}
be the least such that
{\rm crit}(H)=\nu_{\mathfrak{q}}(=\nu=\nu_{\mathfrak{p}})
and
\zeta<{\rm lh}(H)
, and set
{\mathcal{P}}_{H}=Ult({\mathcal{P}},H)
and
{\mathfrak{p}}_{H}=({\mathcal{P}}_{H},\Sigma_{{\mathcal{P}}_{H}})
.
Then
\beta<\pi_{{\mathfrak{p}}_{H},\infty}(\nu_{\mathfrak{p}})
.

11.2.
The proof of Clause 1

We now prove clause 1 of Theorem
11.1
.
Fix a hod pair
{\mathfrak{k}}=({\mathcal{K}},\Psi)
such that
\Psi
has Wadge rank
<\nu^{\infty}=\pi_{{\mathfrak{p}},\infty}(\nu)
in

.
Let

{\mathcal{P}}
-successor of
\nu
.
It is enough to establish the following lemma.
Indeed, varying
{\mathfrak{k}}
, we can find
{\mathfrak{q}}
and
\zeta
as in the lemma such that the Wadge rank of
{\mathfrak{k}}|\zeta
is as high below
\nu^{\infty}
as we wish.

Lemma 11.6

There is a complete iterate

{\sf{Maximality}}:

•

then let

.

(Condition 6)
For all

(Condition 7)
Assume
\epsilon<\iota
is a limit ordinal. Then
{\mathcal{M}}_{\epsilon}
is

. More precisely, for each

.
Fix such an
\epsilon
, and let
{\mathfrak{q}}={\mathfrak{w}}_{\epsilon}=_{def}({\mathcal{Q}},\Sigma_{\mathcal{Q}})
.
It also follows from

, Chapter 8.4]
that, letting

inherits from the background universe, i.e. the strategy induced by

via the resurrection procedure,

.

\Phi
is the same as the fragment of

We observe

(1)

at

This algebra is just like the extender algebra but has

The reason behind (1) is that if

is such that

and

{\mathcal{Q}}

such that

and

.
As

, we have that
{\mathcal{P}}|\delta_{\mathfrak{q}}
satisfies any axiom of the extender algebra that is generated by

.

Let
{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}=({\mathcal{Q}}^{+},\Sigma_{{\mathcal{Q}}^{+}})
be the last model of the
id
-copy of
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}|\delta,{\mathfrak{q}}}
onto
{\mathcal{P}}
,

.
It follows from (1) that
{\mathcal{Q}}^{+}[{\mathcal{P}}|\delta_{\mathfrak{q}}]
makes sense and moreover
\delta_{\mathfrak{q}}
is a regular cardinal of
{\mathcal{Q}}^{+}[{\mathcal{P}}|\delta_{\mathfrak{q}}]
.
It then follows that

(2)
{\mathcal{T}}\in{\mathcal{Q}}^{+}[{\mathcal{P}}|\delta_{\mathfrak{q}}]
.

Indeed,
{\mathcal{T}}
is the output of the least-extender-disagreement-coiteration of
({\mathcal{P}}|\delta,{\mathcal{Q}}|\delta_{\mathfrak{q}})

The branches of

are chosen according to

{\mathcal{P}}|\delta_{\mathfrak{q}}
. Clearly we have that
S^{{\mathcal{P}}|\delta_{\mathfrak{q}}}\in{\mathcal{Q}}^{+}[{\mathcal{P}}|\delta_{\mathfrak{q}}]
.
The next lemma, which establishes a stronger form of maximality, is standard, and its proof can be found in

Lemma 11.7
.

Set
V={\mathcal{P}}
, and suppose
A\subseteq\delta_{\mathfrak{q}}
is such that
\delta_{\mathfrak{q}}
is Woodin relative to
(A,{\mathcal{Q}})
, i.e., for some
\kappa\in w_{\mathfrak{q}}
, for any
{\mathcal{P}}
-cardinal
\mu\in(\kappa,\delta_{\mathfrak{q}})
, there is an
F\in\vec{E}^{\mathcal{P}}
such that
{\rm crit}(F)=\kappa
,
{\rm im}(F)>\mu
,
\pi^{\mathcal{P}}_{F}(A)\cap\mu=A\cap\mu
, and
\pi_{F}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{Q}})|\mu={\mathcal{Q}}|\mu
.
Fix
\kappa<\delta_{\mathfrak{q}}
witnessing that
\delta_{\mathfrak{q}}
is Woodin relative to
(A,{\mathcal{Q}})
.
Then for an unbounded-in-
\delta_{\mathfrak{q}}
set of
{\mathcal{Q}}
-inaccessible cardinals
\mu
, there is an
F\in\vec{E}^{\mathcal{P}}
such that
{\rm crit}(F)=\kappa
,
{\rm im}(F)>\mu
,
\pi_{F}^{\mathcal{P}}(A)\cap\mu=A\cap\mu
,
\pi_{F}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{Q}})|\mu={\mathcal{Q}}|\mu
, and the Jensen completion of
F\cap{\mathcal{Q}}|\mu
is on the sequence of
{\mathcal{Q}}
.

Proof.

Let
\mu\in w_{\mathfrak{q}}
be a measurable cardinal of
{\mathcal{Q}}
108
108

108

Hence at least inaccessible in
V
. See
[
27
]
.
such that
\mu>\kappa
.
As the set of such

is cofinal in

, it is enough to prove that

is as desired. Let

be the least measurable cardinal of
{\mathcal{Q}}
above
\mu
,
\mu^{\prime\prime}
be the least measurable cardinal of
{\mathcal{Q}}
above
\mu^{\prime}
, and
F\in\vec{E}^{{\mathcal{P}}|\delta_{\mathfrak{q}}}
be such that
{\rm crit}(F)=\kappa
,

,
\pi_{F}^{\mathcal{P}}(A)\cap\mu^{\prime}=A\cap\mu^{\prime}
, and
\pi_{F}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{Q}})|\mu^{\prime\prime}={\mathcal{Q}}|\mu^{\prime\prime}
.
Setting
{\mathcal{W}}={\mathcal{Q}}|(\kappa^{+})^{\mathcal{Q}}
, we have that

(3)
{\mathcal{Q}}|\mu^{\prime\prime}\triangleleft\pi_{F}^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{W}})
.

We now want to see that the Jensen completion of
F\cap{\mathcal{Q}}|\mu
is on the extender sequence of
{\mathcal{Q}}
.
In what follows we will abuse our notation and consider the trees
{\mathcal{T}}_{\epsilon}
as trees on
{\mathcal{P}}
.
Notice that we have some
\epsilon<\iota
such that
{\mathcal{W}}={\mathcal{M}}_{\epsilon}
. Let
\epsilon
be such that
{\mathcal{W}}={\mathcal{M}}_{\epsilon}
.
Because

and

{\rm lh}({\mathcal{T}}_{\epsilon})=\kappa+1

Then

.
Let

be the first extender used on the main branch of

at
\kappa

We observe that
G
and

are compatible.
Let

and
\lambda=\pi_{G}^{\mathcal{S}}(\kappa)
.

We claim that
\lambda>\mu
.
Suppose otherwise.
It follows from (3) that
\pi_{G}^{{\mathcal{S}}}({\mathcal{W}})\trianglelefteq{\mathcal{Q}}|\mu
, and so it follows from Condition 6 that

cannot be used in

(in

Thus

, and so

must actually be on the sequence of
{\mathcal{Q}}
(see Condition 6 above).
∎

Now let
{\mathcal{X}}_{0}={\mathcal{T}}^{\nu}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(={\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathfrak{p}},{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu}})

Full normalization implies that

Now
{\mathfrak{k}}
is added by
g_{\xi}=g\cap Coll(\omega,<\xi)
.
The following is our key claim.
Its proof is based on ideas of Woodin.

Claim 11.8
.

{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{\#,\Psi}({\mathcal{P}}|\delta_{\mathfrak{q}}[g_{\xi}])\vDash``\delta_{\mathfrak{q}}
is a Woodin cardinal”.

Proof.

Let
f:\delta_{\mathfrak{q}}\rightarrow\delta_{\mathfrak{q}}
be a function in
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{\#,\Psi}({\mathcal{P}}|\delta_{\mathfrak{q}}[g_{\xi}])
.
Since we can iterate
{\mathcal{Q}}^{+}
above
\delta_{\mathfrak{q}}
to realize
M
as the derived model at the image of
\eta_{\mathfrak{q}}
, and since
\{{\mathcal{X}}_{0},{\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{K}}\}\subseteq{\mathcal{Q}}^{+}[{\mathcal{P}}|\delta_{\mathfrak{q}}[g_{\xi}]]
and (5) holds, we have that

{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{\#,\Psi}({\mathcal{P}}|\delta_{\mathfrak{q}}[g_{\xi}])\in{\mathcal{Q}}^{+}[({\mathcal{P}}|\delta_{\mathfrak{q}})[g_{\xi}]].

Since the extender algebra has
\delta_{\mathfrak{q}}
-cc, there is
h:\delta_{\mathfrak{q}}\rightarrow\delta_{\mathfrak{q}}
in
{\mathcal{Q}}
which dominates
f
in the sense that for every
\rho<\delta_{\mathfrak{q}}
,
f(\rho)<h(\rho)
.
Let
E\in\vec{E}^{\mathcal{Q}}
be an extender on the sequence of
{\mathcal{Q}}
such that
{\rm gen}(E)
is a measurable cardinal of
{\mathcal{Q}}
and if
\kappa={\rm crit}(E)
, then
\pi^{\mathcal{Q}}_{E}(h)(\kappa)<{\rm gen}(E)
.
Then if
F
is the background extender of
E
, then
E
is the Jensen completion of
F\cap{\mathcal{Q}}|{\rm gen}(E)
.
But now we have that
{\rm gen}(E)>\pi_{F}^{\mathcal{P}}(h)(\kappa)>\pi_{F}^{\mathcal{P}}(f)(\kappa)
.
Thus,
F
witnesses the Woodiness of
\delta_{\mathfrak{q}}
for
f
.
∎

Now let
{\mathcal{N}}
be the output of the hod pair construction of
{\mathcal{Q}}^{+}|\delta_{{\mathcal{Q}}}
that is built by using extenders with critical points
>\xi
.

{\sf{Maximality}}
, which we verified in Lemma
11.7
, implies that in the comparison of this construction with
{\mathfrak{k}}
, only the later side moves (e.g. see
[
23
, Lemma 2.11]
).
If the construction side wins the coiteration, then Lemma
11.6
follows as witnessed by
{\mathfrak{q}}
.
On the other hand,
{\mathfrak{k}}
cannot win the coiteration, as this contradicts the universality of the construction.

In order to use universality, however, we need to be more careful. Suppose
{\mathfrak{k}}
iterates past
{\mathcal{N}}
, and let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be the iteration tree according to
\Psi
such that the last model of
{\mathcal{Y}}
extends
{\mathcal{N}}
.
We have that
{\mathcal{Y}}\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{\#,\Psi}({\mathcal{P}}|\delta_{\mathfrak{q}}[g_{\xi}])
, which means that
{\mathcal{Y}}\in{\mathcal{Q}}^{+}[{\mathcal{P}}|\delta_{\mathfrak{q}}[g_{\xi}]]
.
As in all universality proofs, we can now find a continuous chain
\vec{X}=(X_{\epsilon}:\epsilon<\delta_{\mathfrak{q}})
of submodels of
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{\#,\Psi}({\mathcal{P}}|\delta_{\mathfrak{q}}[g_{\xi}])
such that for a club
C
of
\epsilon<\delta_{\mathfrak{q}}
, if
N_{\epsilon}
is the transitive collapse of
X_{\epsilon}
and
\sigma_{\epsilon}:N_{\epsilon}\rightarrow X_{\epsilon}
is the uncollapse map, then
\epsilon={\rm crit}(\sigma_{\epsilon})
and

(6)
\mathscr{P}(\epsilon)\cap N_{\epsilon}=\mathscr{P}(\epsilon)\cap{\mathcal{N}}
.

Lemma
11.7
implies that we can find an
F\in\vec{E}^{{\mathcal{P}}|\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}}
such that, letting
\kappa={\rm crit}(F)
and
\mu={\rm gen}(F)
, the following holds:

(7.1)
\kappa\in C
and
\mu
is an inaccessible cardinal of both
{\mathcal{P}}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}
,

(7.2) the Jensen completion of
F\cap{\mathcal{Q}}|\mu
, call it
E
, is on the extender sequence of
{\mathcal{Q}}
, and

(7.3) for all
\epsilon<\mu
,
N_{\epsilon}
is the transitive collapse of
\pi_{F}^{\mathcal{P}}(\vec{X})_{\epsilon}
.

It then follows that if
\lambda=\min(C-(\kappa+1))
, then
E\cap{\mathcal{N}}|(\lambda^{+})^{\mathcal{N}}
is on the sequence of
{\mathcal{N}}
, witnessing that
\kappa
is a superstrong cardinal.
112
112

112

The details of this argument appear in
[
22
, Claim 4.1]
,
[
40
, Chapters 8.4 and 9]
,
[
23
]
and
[
25
]
. The issue in our case is that we are applying universality to a model built inside a backgrounded universe, and the extra complication arises from the use of Jensen indexing.

∎

12.
The proof of Theorem
8.1

We apply the work of the previous sections to prove Theorem
8.1
.
Notice that the entirety of §
10
is developed under the hypothesis of Theorem
8.1
, as it used the terminology and notation introduced in Notation
10.1
.
Let
M
be as in Theorem
8.1
.
By Corollary
10.17
,

M\vDash{\sf{ZF}}+\text{ ``$\omega_{1}$ is supercompact."}

We now verify that for every
n<\omega
,

M\vDash\Join^{n}_{\lambda}.

It suffices to verify that the following hold in
M
, where
\lambda=\xi^{\infty}
and
\kappa=\Theta^{{\mathrm{N}}_{\lambda}}
:

(1)

the order type of the set
C=\{\tau\in[\kappa,\lambda]:{\mathrm{N}}_{\lambda}\vDash``\tau
is a cardinal
"\}
is at least
n+1
,

(2)

\lambda<\Theta
and
\lambda\in C
,

(3)

\kappa
is a regular member of the Solovay sequence,

(4)

letting
\langle\kappa_{i}:i\leq n\rangle
be the first
n+1
members of
C
enumerated in increasing order,
113
113

113

Thus,
\kappa_{0}=\kappa
.
for every
i\leq n
,
{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}\vDash``\kappa_{i}
is a regular cardinal”,

(5)

{\mathrm{N}}_{\lambda}^{-}\cap\mathscr{P}({\mathbb{R}})={\mathrm{N}}_{\lambda}^{+}\cap\mathscr{P}({\mathbb{R}})=\Delta_{\kappa}
,

(6)

for all
i<n
,
\mathscr{P}(\kappa_{i}^{\omega})\cap{\mathrm{N}}_{\kappa_{i+1}}^{-}=\mathscr{P}(\kappa_{i}^{\omega})\cap{\mathrm{N}}^{+}_{\lambda}
,

(7)

for all
i+1\leq n
,
{\rm cf}(\kappa_{i+1})\geq\kappa
.

Theorem
10.6
and Corollary
10.17
imply (1).
The first part of (2) is a consequence of the fact that in Theorem
8.1
,
\delta<\eta
; the second part follows from Theorem
10.8
.
(3) follows from Theorem
10.20
, and (4) follows from Theorem
10.20
and Theorem
10.23
.
(5) and (6) follow from Theorem
10.13
and Theorem
10.18
.
Theorem
10.26
implies (7).
∎
